 succeeded in 264ms:
     1	//! Topological scheduler — iterative Tarjan SCC over a dirty subset.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per CORR-23 (Week 3 Day 0 deep-read finding): both Formualizer (`engine/scheduler.rs:
     4	//! 88-239`) and HyperFormula (`TopSort.ts:18-92`) use iterative Tarjan SCC rather than
     5	//! Kahn's algorithm for topological scheduling. Reasons:
     6	//!
     7	//! 1. **Cycle detection in the same pass.** Tarjan partitions nodes into SCCs; SCCs of
     8	//!    size 1 with no self-loop are acyclic (go to `sorted`), everything else is cyclic
     9	//!    (goes to `cycled`). Kahn would need a separate "did all dirty nodes emit?" check
    10	//!    after the toposort to identify cycle membership.
    11	//!
    12	//! 2. **Direct API for the Phase 0 cycle policy.** The spec's Phase 0 minimum: cycled
    13	//!    cells become `CellError(Cycle)`; non-cycled cells evaluate in topological order.
    14	//!    The `(sorted, cycled)` partition is exactly what the evaluator wants.
    15	//!
    16	//! 3. **Iterative implementation handles deep graphs.** A 10K-deep dependency chain
    17	//!    would blow the default 8 MB stack with a recursive Tarjan. HyperFormula made the
    18	//!    same choice for JS call-stack reasons; ours is for arbitrary-depth Rust workbooks.
    19	//!
    20	//! 4. **Phase 0 minimum is non-iteration**: cycles are terminal errors, not states to
    21	//!    converge. Full iteration (with a `max_iterations` config) lands Phase 4+ when the
    22	//!    function library includes goal-seek / iterative-solve UDFs.
    23	//!
    24	//! ## Algorithm shape
    25	//!
    26	//! Standard iterative Tarjan with two stacks:
    27	//! - `dfs_stack: Vec<(NodeId, usize)>` — explicit DFS frame: (node, next-child-index).
    28	//!   Replaces the recursion stack of the textbook algorithm.
    29	//! - `scc_stack: Vec<NodeId>` — the Tarjan SCC stack of "currently-being-explored" nodes
    30	//!   that may belong to the same SCC as the current root.
    31	//!
    32	//! Per-node state in `FxHashMap`-equivalent (we use `std::HashMap` to avoid a new dep):
    33	//! - `indices[v]: u32` — DFS discovery order; assigned on first visit.
    34	//! - `lowlinks[v]: u32` — smallest index reachable from v's subtree.
    35	//! - `on_scc_stack[v]: bool` — is v currently on `scc_stack`?
    36	//!
    37	//! When a node's lowlink equals its index after all children are processed, it's the
    38	//! root of an SCC. We pop the SCC stack down to that node.
    39	//!
    40	//! ## Edge filtering
    41	//!
    42	//! The scheduler operates ON the dirty subset only. Edges to non-dirty nodes are skipped
    43	//! (those nodes aren't being re-evaluated; their current values are read as-is). A
    44	//! `dirty: HashSet<NodeId>` membership check filters `graph.outgoing(v)`.
    45	//!
    46	//! ## Output ordering
    47	//!
    48	//! Tarjan emits SCCs in **reverse topological order of the condensation**. For our
    49	//! graph orientation (edge `u → v` means "u depends on v"), this is exactly dependency-
    50	//! first order: when we collect `sorted` by appending SCCs as Tarjan finds them, the
    51	//! result is `[dependencies, ..., dependents]`. Callers evaluate `sorted[0]` first.
    52	
    53	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    54	
    55	use crate::graph::Graph;
    56	use crate::node::NodeId;
    57	
    58	/// The output of a scheduling pass.
    59	///
    60	/// `sorted` is in dependency-first order: evaluate `sorted[0]` first, then `sorted[1]`,
    61	/// etc. `cycled` is unordered — every cycled node should be assigned a cycle error.
    62	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    63	pub struct Schedule {
    64	    pub sorted: Vec<NodeId>,
    65	    pub cycled: Vec<NodeId>,
    66	}
    67	
    68	impl Schedule {
    69	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
    70	        self.sorted.is_empty() && self.cycled.is_empty()
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    pub fn total_count(&self) -> usize {
    74	        self.sorted.len() + self.cycled.len()
    75	    }
    76	}
    77	
    78	/// Schedule the given dirty subset for evaluation. Returns `(sorted, cycled)` where
    79	/// `sorted` is in dependency-first topological order and `cycled` contains all nodes in
    80	/// non-trivial strongly connected components (size > 1) plus all self-loops (size 1 + an
    81	/// edge to self).
    82	///
    83	/// Edges to nodes outside `dirty_subset` are not traversed — those nodes aren't being
    84	/// re-evaluated, just read.
    85	///
    86	/// Duplicate entries in `dirty_subset` are tolerated (deduped internally via a HashSet).
    87	pub fn schedule(graph: &Graph, dirty_subset: &[NodeId]) -> Schedule {
    88	    let dirty: HashSet<NodeId> = dirty_subset.iter().copied().collect();
    89	    if dirty.is_empty() {
    90	        return Schedule::default();
    91	    }
    92	
    93	    let mut indices: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    94	    let mut lowlinks: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    95	    let mut on_scc_stack: HashMap<NodeId, bool> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    96	    let mut scc_stack: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    97	    let mut next_index: u32 = 0;
    98	
    99	    let mut sorted: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   100	    let mut cycled: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   101	
   102	    // Visit every node in the dirty subset. Iteration order is from the input slice (not
   103	    // the HashSet's randomized order) so callers get deterministic schedules from
   104	    // deterministic inputs. Deduplicated via `indices.contains_key`.
   105	    for &seed in dirty_subset {
   106	        if indices.contains_key(&seed) {
   107	            continue;
   108	        }
   109	        tarjan_dfs(
   110	            seed,
   111	            graph,
   112	            &dirty,
   113	            &mut indices,
   114	            &mut lowlinks,
   115	            &mut on_scc_stack,
   116	            &mut scc_stack,
   117	            &mut next_index,
   118	            &mut sorted,
   119	            &mut cycled,
   120	        );
   121	    }
   122	
   123	    Schedule { sorted, cycled }
   124	}
   125	
   126	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   127	fn tarjan_dfs(
   128	    seed: NodeId,
   129	    graph: &Graph,
   130	    dirty: &HashSet<NodeId>,
   131	    indices: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   132	    lowlinks: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   133	    on_scc_stack: &mut HashMap<NodeId, bool>,
   134	    scc_stack: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   135	    next_index: &mut u32,
   136	    sorted: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   137	    cycled: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   138	) {
   139	    // Explicit DFS frame: (node, child index in outgoing(node) — within-dirty filter).
   140	    // We compute the child slice once at first visit + iterate it index-by-index across
   141	    // frames. Filtering "is this child in dirty?" happens at child-visit time so we don't
   142	    // pre-allocate filtered child vectors per node (the hot path under no-cycles is one
   143	    // outgoing-slice scan per node, period).
   144	    let mut dfs_stack: Vec<(NodeId, usize)> = Vec::new();
   145	
   146	    // First visit of seed.
   147	    visit_first(seed, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   148	    dfs_stack.push((seed, 0));
   149	
   150	    while let Some(&(v, child_idx)) = dfs_stack.last() {
   151	        let children = graph.outgoing(v);
   152	
   153	        // Find the next not-yet-considered child that's in the dirty subset.
   154	        let mut next_child: Option<NodeId> = None;
   155	        let mut new_child_idx = child_idx;
   156	        while new_child_idx < children.len() {
   157	            let candidate = children[new_child_idx];
   158	            new_child_idx += 1;
   159	            if dirty.contains(&candidate) {
   160	                next_child = Some(candidate);
   161	                break;
   162	            }
   163	        }
   164	
   165	        // Update this frame's child cursor regardless of whether we recurse or finish.
   166	        let frame_idx = dfs_stack.len() - 1;
   167	        dfs_stack[frame_idx].1 = new_child_idx;
   168	
   169	        if let Some(w) = next_child {
   170	            if !indices.contains_key(&w) {
   171	                // Unvisited dirty child — recurse.
   172	                visit_first(w, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   173	                dfs_stack.push((w, 0));
   174	            } else if *on_scc_stack
   175	                .get(&w)
   176	                .expect("topo invariant: every key in `indices` must also be in `on_scc_stack` — visit_first populates both atomically")
   177	            {
   178	                // Phase 2A.7 audit M13: switched from `unwrap_or(&false)` to `.expect(...)`.
   179	                // The prior fallback silently treated "missing key" as "not on stack",
   180	                // conflating "not yet visited" with "explicitly off-stack" — masking a
   181	                // Tarjan invariant violation. Per no-fallbacks rule, an invariant
   182	                // violation must panic loudly, not produce subtly-wrong SCC results.
   183	                //
   184	                // Back-edge or cross-edge to a node currently on the SCC stack — update
   185	                // v's lowlink with w's index.
   186	                let w_idx = indices[&w];
   187	                let v_low = lowlinks[&v];
   188	                if w_idx < v_low {
   189	                    lowlinks.insert(v, w_idx);
   190	                }
   191	            }
   192	            // Else: w is in a completed SCC; ignore the cross-edge.
   193	        } else {
   194	            // All children processed — post-order completion.
   195	            dfs_stack.pop();
   196	
   197	            // If v is the root of an SCC, pop it off the SCC stack.
   198	            if lowlinks[&v] == indices[&v] {
   199	                let mut scc: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   200	                loop {
   201	                    let w = scc_stack
   202	                        .pop()
   203	                        .expect("scc_stack drained mid-pop — algorithm invariant violated");
   204	                    on_scc_stack.insert(w, false);
   205	                    scc.push(w);
   206	                    if w == v {
   207	                        break;
   208	                    }
   209	                }
   210	
   211	                // Classify the SCC.
   212	                let is_cycle = scc.len() > 1
   213	                    || (scc.len() == 1
   214	                        && graph
   215	                            .outgoing(scc[0])
   216	                            .iter()
   217	                            .any(|&t| t == scc[0] && dirty.contains(&t)));
   218	                if is_cycle {
   219	                    cycled.extend(scc);
   220	                } else {
   221	                    // Single acyclic node — append to sorted.
   222	                    sorted.extend(scc);
   223	                }
   224	            }
   225	
   226	            // Propagate v's lowlink up to its parent (post-order).
   227	            if let Some(&(parent, _)) = dfs_stack.last() {
   228	                let v_low = lowlinks[&v];
   229	                let p_low = lowlinks[&parent];
   230	                if v_low < p_low {
   231	                    lowlinks.insert(parent, v_low);
   232	                }
   233	            }
   234	        }
   235	    }
   236	}
   237	
   238	fn visit_first(
   239	    v: NodeId,
   240	    next_index: &mut u32,
   241	    indices: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   242	    lowlinks: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   243	    on_scc_stack: &mut HashMap<NodeId, bool>,
   244	    scc_stack: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   245	) {
   246	    let idx = *next_index;
   247	    *next_index = next_index
   248	        .checked_add(1)
   249	        .expect("Tarjan next_index overflow u32 — graphs > 4.3B nodes are not supported");
   250	    indices.insert(v, idx);
   251	    lowlinks.insert(v, idx);
   252	    scc_stack.push(v);
   253	    on_scc_stack.insert(v, true);
   254	}
   255	
   256	#[cfg(test)]
   257	mod tests {
   258	    use super::*;
   259	
   260	    /// Helper: ids of `n` cell nodes in row 0, distinct columns.
   261	    fn n_cells(g: &mut Graph, n: u32) -> Vec<NodeId> {
   262	        (0..n).map(|c| g.add_cell_node(0, 0, c)).collect()
   263	    }
   264	
   265	    #[test]
   266	    fn empty_dirty_returns_empty_schedule() {
   267	        let g = Graph::new();
   268	        let s = schedule(&g, &[]);
   269	        assert!(s.is_empty());
   270	        assert_eq!(s.total_count(), 0);
   271	    }
   272	
   273	    #[test]
   274	    fn single_node_no_edges_goes_to_sorted() {
   275	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   276	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   277	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a]);
   278	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![a]);
   279	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   280	    }
   281	
   282	    #[test]
   283	    fn single_node_with_self_loop_goes_to_cycled() {
   284	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   285	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   286	        g.add_edge(a, a);
   287	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a]);
   288	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty());
   289	        assert_eq!(s.cycled, vec![a]);
   290	    }
   291	
   292	    #[test]
   293	    fn linear_chain_yields_dependency_first_order() {
   294	        // A -> B -> C. A depends on B; B depends on C. Eval order: C, B, A.
   295	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   296	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 3);
   297	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   298	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   299	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   300	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c]);
   301	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![c, b, a]);
   302	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   303	    }
   304	
   305	    #[test]
   306	    fn diamond_dependency_orders_correctly() {
   307	        // A -> {B1, B2} -> C. Eval order: C first, then B1+B2 in either order, then A.
   308	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   309	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 4);
   310	        let (a, b1, b2, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2], ids[3]);
   311	        g.add_edge(a, b1);
   312	        g.add_edge(a, b2);
   313	        g.add_edge(b1, c);
   314	        g.add_edge(b2, c);
   315	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b1, b2, c]);
   316	        // C must come first
   317	        assert_eq!(s.sorted[0], c);
   318	        // A must come last
   319	        assert_eq!(s.sorted[3], a);
   320	        // B1 and B2 must come between
   321	        let mid: std::collections::HashSet<_> = [s.sorted[1], s.sorted[2]].into_iter().collect();
   322	        assert_eq!(mid, [b1, b2].into_iter().collect());
   323	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   324	    }
   325	
   326	    #[test]
   327	    fn two_node_cycle_goes_to_cycled() {
   328	        // A <-> B. Both belong to the same SCC of size 2.
   329	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   330	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 2);
   331	        let (a, b) = (ids[0], ids[1]);
   332	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   333	        g.add_edge(b, a);
   334	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b]);
   335	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty());
   336	        assert_eq!(s.cycled.len(), 2);
   337	        let cycled: std::collections::HashSet<_> = s.cycled.iter().copied().collect();
   338	        assert_eq!(cycled, [a, b].into_iter().collect());
   339	    }
   340	
   341	    #[test]
   342	    fn mixed_cycle_and_acyclic_in_same_dirty_subset() {
   343	        // Cycle {A, B} disjoint from acyclic chain C -> D -> E.
   344	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   345	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 5);
   346	        let (a, b, c, d, e) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2], ids[3], ids[4]);
   347	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   348	        g.add_edge(b, a); // cycle
   349	        g.add_edge(c, d);
   350	        g.add_edge(d, e);
   351	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c, d, e]);
   352	        // Cycled has both a and b.
   353	        let cycled: std::collections::HashSet<_> = s.cycled.iter().copied().collect();
   354	        assert_eq!(cycled, [a, b].into_iter().collect());
   355	        // Sorted has [e, d, c] (dependency-first).
   356	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![e, d, c]);
   357	    }
   358	
   359	    #[test]
   360	    fn dirty_subset_filters_edges_to_clean_nodes() {
   361	        // A -> B -> C in graph; dirty subset = [A, B] only. Edge B -> C should be
   362	        // ignored (C is clean / read-as-is).
   363	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   364	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 3);
   365	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   366	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   367	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   368	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b]);
   369	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![b, a]);
   370	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   371	        // C never appears.
   372	        assert!(!s.sorted.contains(&c));
   373	        assert!(!s.cycled.contains(&c));
   374	    }

   376	    #[test]
   377	    fn duplicate_seeds_are_deduped() {
   378	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   379	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   380	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, a, a, a]);
   381	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![a]);
   382	    }
   383	
   384	    #[test]
   385	    fn deep_linear_chain_no_stack_overflow() {
   386	        // 10,000-node linear chain. A recursive Tarjan would blow the 8MB stack ~around
   387	        // depth 8k-16k depending on frame size. The iterative implementation should
   388	        // handle this trivially.
   389	        const N: u32 = 10_000;
   390	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   391	        let ids: Vec<NodeId> = (0..N).map(|i| g.add_cell_node(0, i, 0)).collect();
   392	        for i in 0..(N as usize - 1) {
   393	            g.add_edge(ids[i], ids[i + 1]); // i depends on i+1
   394	        }
   395	        let s = schedule(&g, &ids);
   396	        assert_eq!(s.sorted.len(), N as usize);
   397	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   398	        // Last node (highest index) must come first (it's the deepest dependency).
   399	        assert_eq!(s.sorted[0], ids[(N - 1) as usize]);
   400	        // Source (index 0) must come last.
   401	        assert_eq!(*s.sorted.last().unwrap(), ids[0]);
   402	    }
   403	
   404	    #[test]
   405	    fn three_node_cycle() {
   406	        // A -> B -> C -> A.
   407	        let mut g = Graph::new();

   409	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   410	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   411	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   412	        g.add_edge(c, a);
   413	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c]);
   414	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty());
   415	        assert_eq!(s.cycled.len(), 3);
   416	    }

   421	        // C itself is not in the cycle).
   422	        let mut g = Graph::new();

   438	        // happened to point to a non-dirty version of itself, the cycle detection skips
   439	        // it — but that's a contrived case since `NodeId` identity is global. Test the
   440	        // straightforward case: self-loop + dirty = cycled.
   441	        let mut g = Graph::new();

   449	    fn nodes_not_in_dirty_subset_never_appear_in_output() {
   450	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   451	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 5);
   452	        let (a, b, _c, _d, _e) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2], ids[3], ids[4]);
   453	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   454	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b]);
   455	        assert_eq!(s.total_count(), 2);
   456	        // 3 nodes exist in the graph but only 2 are in the schedule.
   457	        for node in &s.sorted {
   458	            assert!([a, b].contains(node));
   459	        }
   460	    }

codex
The graph contract matches the reported failure mode: direct deps are real adjacency edges, range deps are side tables only, and Tarjan consumes only `graph.outgoing(v)` filtered to the dirty subset. I’m checking the stripe implementation and runtime hook path now, because the interaction between `remove_formula_deps`, dirty fanout, and recompute scheduling is where the decision risk lives.

     1	//! Stripe index — `(sheet, axis, index) → set of dependent NodeIds`. The A5 acceptance
     2	//! piece: given a cell-coordinate write, find all formulas that reference a range
     3	//! containing that cell, in time proportional to the number of distinct stripes touched
     4	//! (not the number of formulas).
     5	//!
     6	//! ## Pattern (CORR-21)
     7	//!
     8	//! Per the Week 3 Day 0 reference deep-read (`docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md`),
     9	//! Formualizer's `engine/graph/range_deps.rs:36-192` is the closest precedent. The
    10	//! pattern:
    11	//!
    12	//! - `StripeKey { sheet_id, stripe_type: Row | Column, index: u32 }`.
    13	//! - `stripe_to_dependents: HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>>`.
    14	//! - At range registration, a **shape heuristic** picks the cheaper axis to index along:
    15	//!   `if height > width → Column stripes`, `else → Row stripes`. So `SUM(A:A)` registers
    16	//!   ONE Column-stripe entry (col 0); `SUM(A1:A1000)` also ONE Column entry; `SUM(A1:Z1)`
    17	//!   ONE Row entry. A range `A1:Z1000` with height==width=tie goes to Row stripes (the
    18	//!   `else` branch).
    19	//!
    20	//! We intentionally **skip** Formualizer's optional 256×256 Block stripes for Phase 0.
    21	//! They add a third stripe axis only when `enable_block_stripes` is set AND the range
    22	//! is 2D; the A5 falsifier doesn't need them. Revisit in Week 4 if A1 acceptance
    23	//! reveals 2D-region perf cliffs.
    24	//!
    25	//! ## Precision-check requirement
    26	//!
    27	//! The stripe map is **coarser** than the range. `SUM(A1:A10)` registers under Column 0,
    28	//! but a write to `A500` would falsely match. Callers MUST cross-check each candidate
    29	//! returned by `dependents_for_cell` against the formula's actual `RangeRef` (stored on
    30	//! `Graph::formula_to_range_deps`) before considering it a true dependent.
    31	//!
    32	//! `Graph::dependents_for_cell` (added in this commit) wraps both steps.
    33	
    34	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    35	
    36	use ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef;
    37	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    38	
    39	use crate::node::NodeId;
    40	
    41	/// Axis discriminator for `StripeKey`. Phase 0 has only Row and Column; Block is
    42	/// deferred (see module doc).
    43	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    44	pub enum StripeType {
    45	    Row,
    46	    Column,
    47	}
    48	
    49	/// A single stripe identifier: `(sheet, axis, index)`. For `StripeType::Row`, `index` is
    50	/// a `RowId`; for `StripeType::Column`, a `ColId`. Stored as `u32` since both type-alias
    51	/// to `u32` upstream.
    52	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    53	pub struct StripeKey {
    54	    pub sheet_id: SheetId,
    55	    pub stripe_type: StripeType,
    56	    pub index: u32,
    57	}
    58	
    59	/// Sparse per-stripe index of which formulas depend on each stripe.
    60	///
    61	/// Use via `Graph::register_range_dependency` and `Graph::dependents_for_cell` — those
    62	/// wrap the stripe insertion and precision-check together.
    63	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    64	pub struct StripeIndex {
    65	    stripe_to_dependents: HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>>,
    66	}
    67	
    68	impl StripeIndex {
    69	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    70	        Self::default()
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    /// Distinct stripe keys present in the map. Used by tests + W3-7 graph-profile export.
    74	    pub fn stripe_count(&self) -> usize {
    75	        self.stripe_to_dependents.len()
    76	    }
    77	
    78	    /// Total `(stripe_key, formula_node)` insertions across all stripes. The A5 acceptance
    79	    /// test asserts this scales linearly with formula count (1 insert per formula via the
    80	    /// shape heuristic), NOT with range size.
    81	    pub fn total_insertions(&self) -> usize {
    82	        self.stripe_to_dependents.values().map(|s| s.len()).sum()
    83	    }
    84	
    85	    /// Test-only read access for A4 structural assertions (CORR-24 typed accessor pattern).
    86	    #[cfg(test)]
    87	    pub(crate) fn raw_map(&self) -> &HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>> {
    88	        &self.stripe_to_dependents
    89	    }
    90	
    91	    /// Register `formula_node`'s dependency on `range`. Picks the cheaper-to-index axis
    92	    /// via the shape heuristic; inserts one entry per touched stripe index along that
    93	    /// axis. Resolves the `RangeRef`'s optional sheet to `sheet_id` if `None`.
    94	    ///
    95	    /// **Panics** if the `range` has reversed bounds (start > end on any axis). Per the
    96	    /// no-fallbacks rule (audit finding H1, 2026-05-12): malformed ranges previously
    97	    /// silently no-op'd the stripe insert because `for c in start..=end` is an empty
    98	    /// range when `start > end`. The formula would have appeared registered (entry in
    99	    /// `formula_to_range_deps`) but be unreachable via writes — silent dependency loss.
   100	    /// Now: loud panic at the boundary. Caller is responsible for normalizing before
   101	    /// calling.
   102	    pub fn register(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId, range: &RangeRef, sheet_id: SheetId) {
   103	        match range {
   104	            RangeRef::Cells {
   105	                sheet,
   106	                start_col,
   107	                start_row,
   108	                end_col,
   109	                end_row,
   110	                ..
   111	            } => {
   112	                assert!(
   113	                    start_col <= end_col,
   114	                    "StripeIndex::register: Cells range has reversed cols (start_col={start_col} > end_col={end_col})"
   115	                );
   116	                assert!(
   117	                    start_row <= end_row,
   118	                    "StripeIndex::register: Cells range has reversed rows (start_row={start_row} > end_row={end_row})"
   119	                );
   120	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   121	                let height = end_row - start_row + 1;
   122	                let width = end_col - start_col + 1;
   123	                if height > width {
   124	                    for c in *start_col..=*end_col {
   125	                        self.insert(
   126	                            StripeKey {
   127	                                sheet_id: s,
   128	                                stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   129	                                index: c,
   130	                            },
   131	                            formula_node,
   132	                        );
   133	                    }
   134	                } else {
   135	                    for r in *start_row..=*end_row {
   136	                        self.insert(
   137	                            StripeKey {
   138	                                sheet_id: s,
   139	                                stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   140	                                index: r,
   141	                            },
   142	                            formula_node,
   143	                        );
   144	                    }
   145	                }
   146	            }
   147	            RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   148	                sheet,
   149	                start_col,
   150	                end_col,
   151	                ..
   152	            } => {
   153	                assert!(
   154	                    start_col <= end_col,
   155	                    "StripeIndex::register: WholeColumn has reversed cols (start_col={start_col} > end_col={end_col})"
   156	                );
   157	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   158	                for c in *start_col..=*end_col {
   159	                    self.insert(
   160	                        StripeKey {
   161	                            sheet_id: s,
   162	                            stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   163	                            index: c,
   164	                        },
   165	                        formula_node,
   166	                    );
   167	                }
   168	            }
   169	            RangeRef::WholeRow {
   170	                sheet,
   171	                start_row,
   172	                end_row,
   173	                ..
   174	            } => {
   175	                assert!(
   176	                    start_row <= end_row,
   177	                    "StripeIndex::register: WholeRow has reversed rows (start_row={start_row} > end_row={end_row})"
   178	                );
   179	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   180	                for r in *start_row..=*end_row {
   181	                    self.insert(
   182	                        StripeKey {
   183	                            sheet_id: s,
   184	                            stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   185	                            index: r,
   186	                        },
   187	                        formula_node,
   188	                    );
   189	                }
   190	            }
   191	        }
   192	    }
   193	
   194	    fn insert(&mut self, key: StripeKey, formula_node: NodeId) {
   195	        self.stripe_to_dependents
   196	            .entry(key)
   197	            .or_default()
   198	            .insert(formula_node);
   199	    }
   200	
   201	    /// Candidate dependents for a cell write at `(sheet, row, col)`. Returns the union of
   202	    /// the Row-stripe-at-row and Column-stripe-at-col bucket contents. **Coarse**:
   203	    /// candidates must be precision-checked against their `formula_to_range_deps` to
   204	    /// drop false positives. `Graph::dependents_for_cell` performs the precision check.
   205	    pub fn candidates_for_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> HashSet<NodeId> {
   206	        let mut out = HashSet::new();
   207	        let row_key = StripeKey {
   208	            sheet_id: sheet,
   209	            stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   210	            index: row,
   211	        };
   212	        if let Some(deps) = self.stripe_to_dependents.get(&row_key) {
   213	            out.extend(deps.iter().copied());
   214	        }
   215	        let col_key = StripeKey {
   216	            sheet_id: sheet,
   217	            stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   218	            index: col,
   219	        };
   220	        if let Some(deps) = self.stripe_to_dependents.get(&col_key) {
   221	            out.extend(deps.iter().copied());
   222	        }
   223	        out
   224	    }
   225	}
   226	
   227	/// Test whether `range` (sheet-resolved) contains the cell at `(row, col)`. The sheet
   228	/// check is implicit (caller already filtered by sheet via the stripe key); this only
   229	/// validates row/col bounds.
   230	pub(crate) fn range_contains_rowcol(range: &RangeRef, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> bool {
   231	    match range {
   232	        RangeRef::Cells {
   233	            start_col,
   234	            start_row,
   235	            end_col,
   236	            end_row,
   237	            ..
   238	        } => row >= *start_row && row <= *end_row && col >= *start_col && col <= *end_col,
   239	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   240	            start_col, end_col, ..
   241	        } => col >= *start_col && col <= *end_col,
   242	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   243	            start_row, end_row, ..
   244	        } => row >= *start_row && row <= *end_row,
   245	    }
   246	}

   432	    /// A5 ACCEPTANCE: register 100k formulas `SUM(A1:A_n)` with `n` varying. Total
   433	    /// stripe entries (sum across all formulas' insertions) ≤ 2 × formula_count.
   434	    ///
   435	    /// With Formualizer's shape heuristic, each `Cells { 0, 0, 0, n-1 }` for n ≥ 2 has
   436	    /// height=n, width=1, h>w → 1 Column stripe at col 0. For n=1 (h==w==1, tie → else
   437	    /// branch) → 1 Row stripe at row 0. So all 100k formulas land in 2 buckets total
   438	    /// (one Column at col 0 + one Row at row 0). `total_insertions == 100_000`.
   439	    ///
   440	    /// The 2× upper bound is the spec's guard against quadratic growth — actual scaling
   441	    /// is far better (linear with formula count).
   442	    #[test]
   443	    fn a5_acceptance_100k_formulas_linear_scaling() {
   444	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   445	        const N: u32 = 100_000;
   446	        for i in 1..=N {
   447	            idx.register(NodeId(i), &cells(0, 0, 0, i - 1), 0);
   448	        }
   449	        // Two distinct stripe keys: Column at col 0 (for n>=2), Row at row 0 (for n=1).
   450	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 2);
   451	        // Exactly N insertions — one per formula.
   452	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), N as usize);
   453	        // The bound from the spec:
   454	        assert!(idx.total_insertions() as u64 <= 2 * (N as u64));
   455	
   456	        // Cell write at A500 → all 100k-1 formulas (except n=1) match the Column stripe.
   457	        // Plus the n=1 formula matches the Row 0 stripe (since 500 is the row but 0 is
   458	        // the row of the n=1 range — wait, candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0) checks Row 500
   459	        // and Column 0. Row 500's stripe is empty; Column 0's stripe has all 99999
   460	        // formulas (n>=2). The n=1 formula registered Row 0, not Row 500, so it
   461	        // doesn't appear in the Row 500 stripe.
   462	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0);
   463	        assert_eq!(candidates.len(), (N - 1) as usize); // n=2 through n=100_000
   464	    }

   467	    fn duplicate_register_idempotent() {
   468	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();

   470	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_col(0, 0), 0); // duplicate
   471	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   472	        // HashSet dedupes; total_insertions counts distinct formulas per stripe.
   473	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 1);
   474	    }

   542	    #[test]
   543	    fn raw_map_accessor_exposes_internals_for_a4() {
   544	        // CORR-24 typed-accessor pattern — A4 assertions read directly off the map.
   545	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   546	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   547	        idx.register(NodeId(2), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   548	        let map = idx.raw_map();
   549	        assert_eq!(map.len(), 1);
   550	        let key = StripeKey {
   551	            sheet_id: 0,
   552	            stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   553	            index: 0,
   554	        };
   555	        assert_eq!(
   556	            map.get(&key).unwrap(),
   557	            &[NodeId(1), NodeId(2)].into_iter().collect::<HashSet<_>>()
   558	        );
   559	    }

 succeeded in 289ms:
     1	//! Engine Phase 3.1+3.2 (2026-05-12) — runtime ↔ calcgraph integration.
     2	//!
     3	//! `CalcgraphSession` owns the `ql_calcgraph::Graph` plus per-formula
     4	//! dependency tracking (cells / ranges / volatile / names). It lives
     5	//! at the engine level (in `ql-exec`) so it can reach
     6	//! `ql-storage::Workbook` for rebuild + `ql-formula-syntax` for
     7	//! lex/parse without dragging those deps into `ql-calcgraph`.
     8	//!
     9	//! ## Phase 3.1 (ownership shell, shipped W5-34)
    10	//!
    11	//! - Single-writer ownership: runtime borrows `&mut CalcgraphSession`
    12	//!   per edit (parallel to `&mut OpLog`).
    13	//! - O(1) `cell_index: HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), NodeId>` for
    14	//!   "which node represents this cell?" lookups.
    15	//! - 5 hooks (`on_set_{value,formula,name}`, `on_clear_formula`,
    16	//!   `on_add_sheet`) wired through `WorkbookRuntime`.
    17	//! - `rebuild_from_workbook` adds a `CellNode` per formula cell in
    18	//!   deterministic `(sheet, row, col)` sort order.
    19	//!
    20	//! ## Phase 3.2 (this commit) — dependency extraction
    21	//!
    22	//! - `walk_plan_for_deps` recursively walks a bound `ExprPlan`,
    23	//!   producing a `FormulaDeps` collection of (a) direct cell refs,
    24	//!   (b) named-range refs (from `AggregateNameRef`), (c) names
    25	//!   referenced (currently only via `AggregateNameRef`; see below),
    26	//!   (d) volatile-function presence.
    27	//! - `on_set_formula` now takes `&ExprPlan` and runs the extraction.
    28	//!   The session updates per-formula `formula_deps`, the volatile
    29	//!   set, and a name→formulas reverse index.
    30	//! - `rebuild_from_workbook` lex+parse+binds every formula and runs
    31	//!   the same extraction. Failures aggregate into
    32	//!   `RebuildResult { attempted, succeeded, failures }` — parallel
    33	//!   to `RecomputeResult` (Phase 2B.2). The session is still returned
    34	//!   on partial failure so the caller can inspect.
    35	//! - Dependency storage is **session-side**, not in the `Graph`. The
    36	//!   Phase 0 `Graph` edges are append-only; tracking the live deps on
    37	//!   the session lets Phase 3.3 implement re-registration cleanly
    38	//!   without needing edge removal (Phase 3.3 may swap in
    39	//!   Formualizer-style delta-edges for the graph too).
    40	//!
    41	//! ## Still deferred to Phase 3.3+
    42	//!
    43	//! - **Dirty propagation.** The 5 mutation hooks bump counters and
    44	//!   maintain dep state but don't yet fan out via
    45	//!   `Graph::dependents_for_cell`. Phase 3.3 wires that.
    46	//! - **Topological recompute.** `recompute_all` still HashMap-order
    47	//!   (GAP-R-01). Phase 3.4 replaces with Tarjan SCC.
    48	//! - **Name-dep tracking from non-AggregateNameRef paths.** When a
    49	//!   `NameRef` resolves to a Cell/Number/Bool/Text target, the binder
    50	//!   substitutes the underlying value and the name is lost. Today
    51	//!   such names invalidate via the PlanCache's `name_gen` counter
    52	//!   (Phase 2B.3) — a workbook-wide invalidation, not per-name.
    53	//!   Phase 3.3 may switch to per-name dirty propagation; until then
    54	//!   the PlanCache covers correctness.
    55	//!
    56	//! ## Ownership model
    57	//!
    58	//! ```text
    59	//!  IDE / caller
    60	//!    │
    61	//!    ├─ owns Workbook                      (storage)
    62	//!    ├─ owns OpLog                         (history; optional)
    63	//!    ├─ owns CalcgraphSession              (graph; optional, new in 3.1)
    64	//!    └─ owns FunctionRegistry              (process-wide singleton-ish)
    65	//!         │
    66	//!         └─ constructs WorkbookRuntime per edit (per-edit borrow window)
    67	//! ```
    68	//!
    69	//! Phase 6.1 `WorkbookSession` will absorb Workbook + OpLog +
    70	//! CalcgraphSession + PlanCache + FunctionRegistry into ONE owning
    71	//! struct that the binding crate can wrap cleanly (per GAP-PS-09).
    72	//! Today they're separate to keep refactor scope bounded.
    73	
    74	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    75	use std::sync::Arc;
    76	
    77	use ql_calcgraph::{schedule, CellNode, Graph, Node, NodeId, Schedule};
    78	use ql_formula_syntax::{lex, parse, RangeRef};
    79	use ql_storage::Workbook;
    80	use ql_types::{ColId, Range, RowId, SheetId};
    81	
    82	use crate::aggregate_cache::{AggregateCacheStats, InMemAggregateCache};
    83	use crate::plan::{bind_with_names, ExprPlan};
    84	use crate::workbook_runtime::RuntimeError;
    85	
    86	/// Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12) — convert a `ql_types::Range` (used in
    87	/// `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef`) to a `ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef`
    88	/// (used by `Graph::register_range_dependency`). The conversion picks
    89	/// the most precise variant available so the stripe index stays
    90	/// efficient:
    91	///
    92	/// - `start_row == 0 && end_row == RowId::MAX` → `WholeColumn` (one
    93	///   stripe per column in the range).
    94	/// - `start_col == 0 && end_col == ColId::MAX` → `WholeRow` (one
    95	///   stripe per row).
    96	/// - Otherwise → `Cells` (StripeIndex picks the smaller axis).
    97	///
    98	/// The Phase 0 `StripeIndex::register` panics on reversed bounds; this
    99	/// helper trusts `Range` to be normalized (per `Range::new` which
   100	/// sorts via `min/max`).
   101	fn range_to_rangeref(r: Range) -> RangeRef {
   102	    let whole_rows = r.start_row == 0 && r.end_row == RowId::MAX;
   103	    let whole_cols = r.start_col == 0 && r.end_col == ColId::MAX;
   104	    if whole_rows && !whole_cols {
   105	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   106	            sheet: Some(r.sheet),
   107	            start_col: r.start_col,
   108	            end_col: r.end_col,
   109	            abs_start: false,
   110	            abs_end: false,
   111	        }
   112	    } else if whole_cols && !whole_rows {
   113	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   114	            sheet: Some(r.sheet),
   115	            start_row: r.start_row,
   116	            end_row: r.end_row,
   117	            abs_start: false,
   118	            abs_end: false,
   119	        }
   120	    } else {
   121	        // Bounded rectangle (or fully-saturated rectangle, which the
   122	        // stripe index handles by picking the smaller axis).
   123	        RangeRef::Cells {
   124	            sheet: Some(r.sheet),
   125	            start_col: r.start_col,
   126	            start_row: r.start_row,
   127	            end_col: r.end_col,
   128	            end_row: r.end_row,
   129	            abs_start_col: false,
   130	            abs_start_row: false,
   131	            abs_end_col: false,
   132	            abs_end_row: false,
   133	        }
   134	    }
   135	}
   136	
   137	/// Phase 3.2 (2026-05-12) — hardcoded list of functions whose RESULT
   138	/// depends on something other than their arguments (NOW changes every
   139	/// recompute even with no inputs; INDIRECT reads a cell named by a
   140	/// string at runtime). The Phase 3.7 volatile-invalidation pass will
   141	/// use this set to schedule volatile re-evaluation. Tracked here as
   142	/// V0; Engine Phase 4.3 (function library expansion) replaces with
   143	/// per-function metadata on `FunctionRegistry`.
   144	///
   145	/// Names MUST be canonical upper-case (matches the parser's
   146	/// canonicalization for `Expr::Function::name`).
   147	pub(crate) fn is_volatile_function(name: &str) -> bool {
   148	    matches!(
   149	        name,
   150	        // Time / date — value changes every recompute.
   151	        "NOW" | "TODAY"
   152	        // Pseudo-random — value changes every recompute.
   153	        | "RAND" | "RANDBETWEEN" | "RANDARRAY"
   154	        // Address-by-string — result depends on workbook structure;
   155	        // a cell rename anywhere can affect the result.
   156	        | "INDIRECT"
   157	        // Structural offset — result depends on the current grid.
   158	        | "OFFSET"
   159	        // Environment introspection.
   160	        | "INFO" | "CELL"
   161	    )
   162	}
   163	
   164	/// Phase 3.2 — direct-dep collection extracted from walking a bound
   165	/// `ExprPlan`. Fields are owned (no borrows) so the caller can pass
   166	/// the collection around or store it without lifetime ceremony.
   167	///
   168	/// `cells`, `named_ranges`, and `names` are deduplicated by the
   169	/// `extract_and_register_deps` orchestrator (the walker emits
   170	/// duplicates; the orchestrator collapses).
   171	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
   172	pub struct FormulaDeps {
   173	    /// Direct cell references (`ExprPlan::CellRef`). Resolved sheet
   174	    /// is included.
   175	    pub cells: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)>,
   176	    /// Named-range references (`ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef`). Each
   177	    /// entry pairs the canonical (uppercase) name with the resolved
   178	    /// `Range` payload.
   179	    pub named_ranges: Vec<(Arc<str>, Range)>,
   180	    /// Names referenced by the formula. Currently only names that
   181	    /// resolve to ranges populate this (via `AggregateNameRef`); see
   182	    /// module docs for the limitation around scalar-resolved names.
   183	    pub names: Vec<Arc<str>>,
   184	    /// `true` iff any volatile function appears anywhere in the
   185	    /// plan tree.
   186	    pub is_volatile: bool,
   187	}
   188	
   189	impl FormulaDeps {
   190	    /// Total dependency count (cells + named ranges). Useful for
   191	    /// summary metrics; Phase 3.10 megaudit / ql-profile will surface
   192	    /// per-formula dep counts in the graph-profile JSON.
   193	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   194	        self.cells.len() + self.named_ranges.len()
   195	    }
   196	
   197	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   198	        self.cells.is_empty() && self.named_ranges.is_empty()
   199	    }
   200	}
   201	
   202	/// Phase 3.2 — recursive walker. Accumulates dependencies + volatile-
   203	/// function presence by descending the `ExprPlan` tree. Does not
   204	/// allocate any nodes or touch any graph state; pure read.
   205	pub(crate) fn walk_plan_for_deps(plan: &ExprPlan, deps: &mut FormulaDeps) {
   206	    match plan {
   207	        ExprPlan::Number(_) | ExprPlan::Bool(_) | ExprPlan::String(_) => {
   208	            // Leaf literals carry no dependencies.
   209	        }
   210	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
   211	            sheet, row, col, ..
   212	        } => {
   213	            deps.cells.push((*sheet, *row, *col));
   214	        }
   215	        ExprPlan::Binary { lhs, rhs, .. } => {
   216	            walk_plan_for_deps(lhs, deps);
   217	            walk_plan_for_deps(rhs, deps);
   218	        }
   219	        ExprPlan::Unary { operand, .. } => {
   220	            walk_plan_for_deps(operand, deps);
   221	        }
   222	        ExprPlan::Function { name, args } => {
   223	            if is_volatile_function(name) {
   224	                deps.is_volatile = true;
   225	            }
   226	            for arg in args {
   227	                walk_plan_for_deps(arg, deps);
   228	            }
   229	        }
   230	        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { name, range } => {
   231	            deps.named_ranges.push((Arc::clone(name), *range));
   232	            deps.names.push(Arc::clone(name));
   233	        }
   234	    }
   235	}
   236	
   237	/// Phase 3.1 runtime ↔ calcgraph integration container. Owns the
   238	/// `Graph` plus an O(1) cell index. Construct via `new()` for empty,
   239	/// or `rebuild_from_workbook` to build from an existing workbook's
   240	/// formulas.
   241	///
   242	/// Single-writer ownership: the runtime borrows `&mut CalcgraphSession`
   243	/// for the lifetime of one edit (same pattern as `&mut OpLog`).
   244	#[derive(Debug, Default)]
   245	pub struct CalcgraphSession {
   246	    graph: Graph,
   247	    /// O(1) lookup from a cell address to its `NodeId`. The Phase 0
   248	    /// `Graph` itself doesn't carry this index — it's a runtime-side
   249	    /// concern (the bench / structural tests don't need it). 3.1 puts
   250	    /// it here so the runtime hooks can answer "do I already have a
   251	    /// node for this cell?" without scanning.
   252	    cell_index: HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), NodeId>,
   253	    /// Phase 3.2: per-formula live dependency view. Keyed by the
   254	    /// formula cell's `NodeId`; replaced wholesale on re-bind so
   255	    /// stale entries can't leak (the Phase 0 graph's edges are
   256	    /// append-only; this side-table sidesteps that until Phase 3.3
   257	    /// brings delta-edge storage in).
   258	    formula_deps: HashMap<NodeId, FormulaDeps>,
   259	    /// Phase 3.2: formulas marked volatile by `walk_plan_for_deps`.
   260	    /// Phase 3.7 (volatile invalidation) will read this set every

 succeeded in 284ms:
   261	    /// recompute cycle to schedule re-evaluation regardless of
   262	    /// upstream dependency state.
   263	    volatile_formulas: HashSet<NodeId>,
   264	    /// Phase 3.2: reverse index from canonical name → formulas that
   265	    /// reference it. Today populated only by `AggregateNameRef` (the
   266	    /// only plan variant that preserves the name after binding). Used
   267	    /// in Phase 3.3 to mark formulas dirty when a named range
   268	    /// changes.
   269	    name_to_formulas: HashMap<Arc<str>, HashSet<NodeId>>,
   270	    /// Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12): reverse index from a cell address to
   271	    /// the formula node-ids that hold a DIRECT cell reference to it
   272	    /// (i.e. `ExprPlan::CellRef`). Range-dep candidates go through
   273	    /// `graph.dependents_for_cell` (stripe + precision); direct cell
   274	    /// deps live here so re-binding a formula can drop stale entries
   275	    /// cleanly without needing edge removal on the append-only Phase 0
   276	    /// `Graph`. Symmetric to `name_to_formulas` for the named-dep path.
   277	    cell_to_formulas: HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), HashSet<NodeId>>,
   278	    /// Phase 3.3: set of formula nodes that need to be recomputed.
   279	    /// Populated by the 5 mutation hooks via stripe + reverse-index
   280	    /// fanout; consumed by `take_dirty` (the future Phase 3.4 Tarjan
   281	    /// SCC scheduler will iterate this set in topological order).
   282	    /// Per-recompute-cycle scoped — the caller clears the set after
   283	    /// recompute.
   284	    dirty: HashSet<NodeId>,
   285	    /// Phase 3.6 (W5-39, 2026-05-12) — AGG-3-01..04 cache. Stores
   286	    /// per-`(range, function_name)` aggregate results so a recompute
   287	    /// that didn't touch any cell inside the range returns the cached
   288	    /// value without re-scanning. `mark_dirty_from_cell_write`
   289	    /// invalidates entries whose range contains the written cell
   290	    /// (matching the stripe-precision-check pattern at the read side).
   291	    aggregate_cache: InMemAggregateCache,
   292	    /// Cumulative observability counters. Phase 3.1 records hook
   293	    /// invocations for verification and future ql-profile integration.
   294	    hook_counts: HookCounts,
   295	}
   296	
   297	/// Cumulative counts of each mutation hook invocation, since session
   298	/// construction. Phase 3.1 ships this for testability + ql-profile
   299	/// observability (Phase 3.10 megaudit will wire it into the
   300	/// graph-profile JSON).
   301	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
   302	pub struct HookCounts {
   303	    pub set_value: u64,
   304	    pub set_formula: u64,
   305	    pub clear_formula: u64,
   306	    pub set_name: u64,
   307	    pub add_sheet: u64,
   308	}
   309	
   310	/// Phase 3.2: aggregate outcome of `rebuild_from_workbook`. Parallel
   311	/// to `RecomputeResult` from Phase 2B.2 — failures are aggregated
   312	/// per-cell rather than short-circuiting, so the caller gets a
   313	/// session containing as much as could be built plus a list of
   314	/// formulas that couldn't be processed.
   315	///
   316	/// `attempted` and `succeeded` are workbook-formula counts; `failures`
   317	/// lists each formula that couldn't lex/parse/bind, carrying its
   318	/// position and the underlying `RuntimeError`. The returned
   319	/// `CalcgraphSession` is usable; cells whose formulas failed simply
   320	/// have a CellNode but no extracted dependencies.
   321	#[derive(Debug)]
   322	pub struct RebuildResult {
   323	    pub session: CalcgraphSession,
   324	    pub attempted: usize,
   325	    pub succeeded: usize,
   326	    pub failures: Vec<RebuildFailure>,
   327	}
   328	
   329	/// Single-formula failure during rebuild. Captures everything the
   330	/// IDE needs to display a per-cell diagnostic without re-walking the
   331	/// workbook.
   332	#[derive(Debug)]
   333	pub struct RebuildFailure {
   334	    pub sheet: SheetId,
   335	    pub row: RowId,
   336	    pub col: ColId,
   337	    pub formula_text: Arc<str>,
   338	    pub error: RuntimeError,
   339	}
   340	
   341	impl RebuildResult {
   342	    pub fn is_complete(&self) -> bool {
   343	        self.failures.is_empty()
   344	    }
   345	    pub fn failed_count(&self) -> usize {
   346	        self.failures.len()
   347	    }
   348	}
   349	
   350	impl CalcgraphSession {
   351	    /// Empty session: empty graph, empty cell index, zero counts.
   352	    pub fn new() -> Self {
   353	        Self::default()
   354	    }
   355	
   356	    /// Borrow the underlying `Graph` (read-only). Used by tests and by
   357	    /// the eventual `ql-profile` graph-profile export.
   358	    pub fn graph(&self) -> &Graph {
   359	        &self.graph
   360	    }
   361	
   362	    /// `NodeId` representing the cell at `(sheet, row, col)`, if any.
   363	    /// `None` if the cell has never been touched by the runtime nor
   364	    /// added by rebuild.
   365	    pub fn cell_node_for(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Option<NodeId> {
   366	        self.cell_index.get(&(sheet, row, col)).copied()
   367	    }
   368	
   369	    /// Get the existing `NodeId` for this cell, or create a new
   370	    /// `CellNode` and return its id. The cell index is updated in
   371	    /// either case.
   372	    fn or_insert_cell_node(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> NodeId {
   373	        if let Some(id) = self.cell_index.get(&(sheet, row, col)) {
   374	            return *id;
   375	        }
   376	        let id = self.graph.add_cell_node(sheet, row, col);
   377	        self.cell_index.insert((sheet, row, col), id);
   378	        id
   379	    }
   380	
   381	    /// Cumulative hook-invocation counters. Phase 3.1 uses these for
   382	    /// observability and tests; Phase 3.10 megaudit wires them into
   383	    /// `ql_profile::Timings` alongside `bind_plan_cache_*` and
   384	    /// `fingerprint_cache_*`.
   385	    pub fn hook_counts(&self) -> HookCounts {
   386	        self.hook_counts
   387	    }
   388	
   389	    /// Phase 3.2: live dependency view for a formula cell. `None` if
   390	    /// the cell has no formula or hasn't been bound yet (rebuild
   391	    /// failed to lex/parse, or the cell isn't a formula at all).
   392	    pub fn formula_deps(&self, node: NodeId) -> Option<&FormulaDeps> {
   393	        self.formula_deps.get(&node)
   394	    }
   395	
   396	    /// Phase 3.2: is this formula's plan marked volatile? Volatile
   397	    /// formulas are scheduled for re-evaluation on every recompute
   398	    /// cycle in Phase 3.7.
   399	    pub fn is_volatile(&self, node: NodeId) -> bool {
   400	        self.volatile_formulas.contains(&node)
   401	    }
   402	
   403	    /// Phase 3.2: read-only view of the volatile-formula set. Phase
   404	    /// 3.7 reads this every recompute cycle.
   405	    pub fn volatile_formulas(&self) -> &HashSet<NodeId> {
   406	        &self.volatile_formulas
   407	    }
   408	
   409	    /// Phase 3.2: formulas that reference `name` (canonical
   410	    /// uppercase). Today populated only from `AggregateNameRef`
   411	    /// occurrences. Phase 3.3 reads this from `on_set_name` to mark
   412	    /// dependents dirty.
   413	    pub fn formulas_referencing_name(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&HashSet<NodeId>> {
   414	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   415	        self.name_to_formulas.get(upper.as_str())
   416	    }
   417	
   418	    /// Phase 3.2 orchestrator + Phase 3.3 graph registration: walk a
   419	    /// bound `ExprPlan`, populate the session's per-formula dep view +
   420	    /// volatile set + name→formulas reverse index + cell→formulas
   421	    /// reverse index (Phase 3.3) + the Graph's stripe-index ranges
   422	    /// (Phase 3.3). Replaces any prior entry for the same formula
   423	    /// node wholesale so re-bind cleanly drops stale session-side
   424	    /// deps even though the Phase 0 graph's edges are append-only.
   425	    ///
   426	    /// Stripe-side stale entries DO accumulate on re-bind (the Phase 0
   427	    /// `Graph` is append-only) — `Graph::dependents_for_cell` filters
   428	    /// these via the formula_to_range_deps precision check, so the
   429	    /// false-positive cost is read-side, not write-side. Phase 3.3
   430	    /// accepts this trade-off (the Phase 0 graph contract); a future
   431	    /// phase may add delta-edges to the graph.
   432	    ///
   433	    /// Internal — public callers go through `on_set_formula` or
   434	    /// rebuild. `formula_node` MUST already exist in the graph (call
   435	    /// `or_insert_cell_node` first if necessary). `formula_sheet` is
   436	    /// the owning sheet of the formula cell; it's the fallback the
   437	    /// `StripeIndex` uses when a range's own `sheet` is `None` (today
   438	    /// every named-range conversion sets `Some(_)` so the fallback is
   439	    /// inert, but the parameter is part of the stable graph API).
   440	    fn extract_and_register_deps(
   441	        &mut self,
   442	        formula_node: NodeId,
   443	        formula_sheet: SheetId,
   444	        plan: &ExprPlan,
   445	    ) {
   446	        // First, evict any prior deps for this formula. Required so
   447	        // re-binding a formula whose text changed (e.g. `=A1` → `=B1`)
   448	        // doesn't leave the old cell-dep stamped on the session.
   449	        self.remove_formula_deps(formula_node);
   450	
   451	        // Walk and collect.
   452	        let mut deps = FormulaDeps::default();
   453	        walk_plan_for_deps(plan, &mut deps);
   454	
   455	        // Register: deduplicate (the walker emits duplicates if the
   456	        // formula references the same cell twice; the session stores
   457	        // each cell once).
   458	        let mut seen_cells: HashSet<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = HashSet::new();
   459	        deps.cells.retain(|c| seen_cells.insert(*c));
   460	        let mut seen_names: HashSet<Arc<str>> = HashSet::new();
   461	        deps.names.retain(|n| seen_names.insert(Arc::clone(n)));
   462	        // named_ranges may contain the same name twice — that's
   463	        // legitimate (`SUM(Sales) + AVERAGE(Sales)` references the
   464	        // same range from two arg positions) — leave as-is.
   465	
   466	        // Phase 3.3: register direct cell deps on the session-side
   467	        // reverse index. We do NOT create CellNodes for dep cells (no
   468	        // need — the lookup at `on_set_value` is by (sheet, row, col)
   469	        // tuple, not by NodeId).
   470	        //
   471	        // Phase 3.4: ALSO add a forward `graph.add_edge(formula_node,
   472	        // dep_node)` when the dep cell is ITSELF a formula cell. The
   473	        // Tarjan scheduler walks `graph.outgoing(formula_node)` to
   474	        // discover the dep set; without this edge it can't order a
   475	        // chain like `=A1`, `=B1=A1+1`, `=C1=B1+1` correctly. The
   476	        // edge is append-only per Phase 0 contract (re-bind leaves
   477	        // stale edges — filed as GAP-G-01; impact is performance,
   478	        // not correctness, because stale edges only add constraints).
   479	        for &(s, r, c) in &deps.cells {
   480	            self.cell_to_formulas
   481	                .entry((s, r, c))
   482	                .or_default()
   483	                .insert(formula_node);
   484	            if let Some(&dep_node) = self.cell_index.get(&(s, r, c)) {
   485	                // Note: self-edges (formula_node == dep_node) are
   486	                // ALLOWED here — they're the canonical way to model
   487	                // `=A1` at A1 (self-cycle), and the Phase 0 W3-3
   488	                // Tarjan scheduler treats a self-looped single-node
   489	                // SCC as `cycled`.
   490	                self.graph.add_edge(formula_node, dep_node);
   491	            }
   492	        }
   493	        // Phase 3.3: register named-range deps with the Graph's stripe
   494	        // index. The Graph's `register_range_dependency` populates BOTH
   495	        // the stripe map AND the `formula_to_range_deps` precision-check
   496	        // map atomically. We pass the formula's owning sheet as the
   497	        // fallback (today every conversion sets `range.sheet =
   498	        // Some(_)`, so the fallback is unused).
   499	        for (_, range) in &deps.named_ranges {
   500	            let range_ref = range_to_rangeref(*range);
   501	            self.graph
   502	                .register_range_dependency(formula_node, range_ref, formula_sheet);
   503	        }
   504	
   505	        if deps.is_volatile {
   506	            self.volatile_formulas.insert(formula_node);
   507	        }
   508	        for name in &deps.names {
   509	            self.name_to_formulas
   510	                .entry(Arc::clone(name))
   511	                .or_default()
   512	                .insert(formula_node);
   513	        }
   514	        if !deps.is_empty() || deps.is_volatile {
   515	            self.formula_deps.insert(formula_node, deps);
   516	        }
   517	    }
   518	
   519	    /// Internal: remove all session-side dep state for `formula_node`.
   520	    /// Used when a formula is re-bound (different text → different
   521	    /// deps) or cleared. Cleans `formula_deps`, the volatile set, the
   522	    /// name→formulas reverse index, and the cell→formulas reverse
   523	    /// index. Does NOT touch the Phase 0 `Graph`'s stripe state or
   524	    /// edges (append-only contract).
   525	    fn remove_formula_deps(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
   526	        if let Some(prior) = self.formula_deps.remove(&formula_node) {
   527	            for name in &prior.names {
   528	                if let Some(set) = self.name_to_formulas.get_mut(name) {
   529	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   530	                    if set.is_empty() {
   531	                        self.name_to_formulas.remove(name);
   532	                    }
   533	                }
   534	            }
   535	            for cell in &prior.cells {
   536	                if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get_mut(cell) {
   537	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   538	                    if set.is_empty() {
   539	                        self.cell_to_formulas.remove(cell);
   540	                    }
   541	                }
   542	            }
   543	        }
   544	        self.volatile_formulas.remove(&formula_node);
   545	    }
   546	
   547	    /// **G3-01 acceptance.** Deterministic rebuild from an existing
   548	    /// `Workbook`. Walks every formula cell in sorted `(sheet, row, col)`
   549	    /// order, adds a `CellNode` per cell, populates the cell index,
   550	    /// and (Phase 3.2) lex+parse+binds each formula to extract
   551	    /// dependencies.
   552	    ///
   553	    /// Determinism: same workbook → same node IDs in the same order.
   554	    /// Workbook's underlying `formula_cells` is a `HashMap` (arbitrary
   555	    /// iteration order), so we sort before adding nodes.
   556	    ///
   557	    /// Failures aggregate into [`RebuildResult::failures`] rather than
   558	    /// short-circuiting — the returned session is usable, just with
   559	    /// some formulas missing dep info. Matches Phase 2B.2's
   560	    /// `RecomputeResult` shape.
   561	    pub fn rebuild_from_workbook(wb: &Workbook) -> RebuildResult {
   562	        let mut session = Self::new();
   563	
   564	        // Snapshot + sort the formula list for determinism.
   565	        let mut formulas: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> = wb
   566	            .iter_formulas()
   567	            .map(|(s, r, c, f)| (s, r, c, Arc::clone(f)))
   568	            .collect();
   569	        formulas.sort_by_key(|a| (a.0, a.1, a.2));
   570	
   571	        let attempted = formulas.len();
   572	        let mut succeeded = 0;
   573	        let mut failures: Vec<RebuildFailure> = Vec::new();
   574	
   575	        // Phase 3.4: pre-pass — insert a CellNode for every formula
   576	        // before any extract runs. The Tarjan-side forward edges
   577	        // require `cell_index` to be COMPLETE at extract time so
   578	        // formula `=A1` at C1 can see A1's node and add the edge
   579	        // (otherwise late-arriving deps would be missed). Done in
   580	        // sorted order so node IDs stay deterministic.
   581	        for (sheet, row, col, _) in &formulas {
   582	            session.or_insert_cell_node(*sheet, *row, *col);
   583	        }
   584	
   585	        for (sheet, row, col, text) in formulas {
   586	            let node = session
   587	                .cell_node_for(sheet, row, col)
   588	                .expect("pre-pass inserted every formula's node");
   589	
   590	            // Phase 3.2: lex + parse + bind + extract deps. Failure
   591	            // accumulates; we keep going. Phase 3.3 passes the
   592	            // formula's owning sheet so the stripe register can use
   593	            // the correct fallback for any range with `sheet: None`.
   594	            match Self::bind_text(&text, sheet, wb) {
   595	                Ok(plan) => {
   596	                    session.extract_and_register_deps(node, sheet, &plan);
   597	                    succeeded += 1;
   598	                }
   599	                Err(error) => failures.push(RebuildFailure {
   600	                    sheet,
   601	                    row,
   602	                    col,
   603	                    formula_text: text,
   604	                    error,
   605	                }),
   606	            }
   607	        }
   608	        // Phase 3.3: a fresh rebuild starts with NO dirty formulas —
   609	        // the caller has presumably already evaluated every cell into
   610	        // the workbook (e.g. `load_workbook_and_recompute`), so the
   611	        // session is in a clean state.
   612	        session.dirty.clear();
   613	        // Phase 3.6: aggregate cache also starts empty. Even if the
   614	        // caller pre-populated the workbook with computed values, those
   615	        // values aren't keyed by `(range, function)` and don't unlock
   616	        // a cache hit on next eval. `clear_all` is a no-op on a fresh
   617	        // session (the Default impl initializes empty), but keeping
   618	        // it makes the contract explicit.
   619	        session.aggregate_cache.clear_all();
   620	
   621	        RebuildResult {
   622	            session,
   623	            attempted,
   624	            succeeded,
   625	            failures,
   626	        }
   627	    }
   628	
   629	    /// Internal: lex + parse + bind a formula text against the
   630	    /// workbook's NameTable. Used by `rebuild_from_workbook` and by
   631	    /// `on_set_formula`'s text-only fallback. Phase 3.3 may add a
   632	    /// cached version that integrates with `PlanCache`.
   633	    fn bind_text(
   634	        text: &str,
   635	        owning_sheet: SheetId,
   636	        wb: &Workbook,
   637	    ) -> Result<ExprPlan, RuntimeError> {
   638	        let tokens = lex(text)?;
   639	        let expr = parse(tokens)?;
   640	        Ok(bind_with_names(&expr, owning_sheet, wb.names())?)
   641	    }
   642	
   643	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 1/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   644	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_value`. Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12): the hook
   645	    /// now marks every formula that depends on `(sheet, row, col)`
   646	    /// dirty via two parallel paths:
   647	    ///
   648	    /// 1. Range deps via `Graph::dependents_for_cell` — stripe lookup
   649	    ///    + precision check (DIR-3-02 acceptance).
   650	    /// 2. Direct cell deps via the session-side `cell_to_formulas`
   651	    ///    reverse index (formulas that hold an `ExprPlan::CellRef` to
   652	    ///    this address).
   653	    ///
   654	    /// The hook DOES NOT create a node for the written cell: nodes
   655	    /// exist for FORMULA cells only; dep lookups for plain literal
   656	    /// cells go through the (sheet, row, col) tuple in both indices.
   657	    pub fn on_set_value(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   658	        self.hook_counts.set_value = self.hook_counts.set_value.saturating_add(1);
   659	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   660	    }
   661	
   662	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 2/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   663	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula`. Ensures the cell has a node,
   664	    /// walks the bound plan to register cell + range + name + volatile
   665	    /// dependencies (Phase 3.2), AND propagates dirty to downstream
   666	    /// formulas (Phase 3.3) — set_formula at a cell that other
   667	    /// formulas already reference means those downstream formulas now
   668	    /// see a different value and must recompute.
   669	    ///
   670	    /// The runtime passes the already-bound `ExprPlan` it just
   671	    /// evaluated; this hook does NOT re-bind. The plan shape is stable
   672	    /// since 2B.4 (Phase 3.2 doesn't add new variants).
   673	    pub fn on_set_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId, plan: &ExprPlan) {
   674	        self.hook_counts.set_formula = self.hook_counts.set_formula.saturating_add(1);
   675	        // Phase 3.4: distinguish first-time-formula at this cell vs
   676	        // re-bind. The first case needs RETROACTIVE forward edges
   677	        // from previously-existing formulas that already reference
   678	        // this cell (those formulas' extract didn't see a NodeId for
   679	        // this cell at the time, so no edge was added).
   680	        let is_new_formula = self.cell_node_for(sheet, row, col).is_none();
   681	        let node = self.or_insert_cell_node(sheet, row, col);
   682	        if is_new_formula {
   683	            let referencing: Vec<NodeId> = self
   684	                .cell_to_formulas
   685	                .get(&(sheet, row, col))
   686	                .map(|s| s.iter().copied().collect())
   687	                .unwrap_or_default();
   688	            for prev in referencing {
   689	                if prev != node {
   690	                    self.graph.add_edge(prev, node);
   691	                }
   692	            }
   693	        }
   694	        self.extract_and_register_deps(node, sheet, plan);
   695	        // Phase 3.3: dirty downstream — formulas referencing the cell
   696	        // whose formula text just changed see a (potentially) new
   697	        // value. The formula itself is NOT marked dirty (the runtime
   698	        // wrote a fresh value for it before invoking this hook).
   699	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   700	    }
   701	
   702	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 3/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   703	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::clear_formula`. Phase 3.2: drops the cell's
   704	    /// session-side dep state (formula_deps, volatile, name reverse
   705	    /// index, cell reverse index). Phase 3.3: also marks downstream
   706	    /// formulas dirty (the cleared formula no longer produces its
   707	    /// prior value; dependents must recompute) and removes the
   708	    /// cleared formula itself from the dirty set.
   709	    pub fn on_clear_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   710	        self.hook_counts.clear_formula = self.hook_counts.clear_formula.saturating_add(1);
   711	        if let Some(node) = self.cell_index.get(&(sheet, row, col)).copied() {
   712	            self.remove_formula_deps(node);
   713	            // The cleared formula doesn't compute anymore. If it was
   714	            // dirty (queued for recompute), drop it from the set.
   715	            self.dirty.remove(&node);
   716	        }
   717	        // Downstream formulas see this cell change — propagate dirty.
   718	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   719	    }
   720	
   721	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 4/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   722	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_name`. Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12): marks all
   723	    /// formulas referencing `name` (per `name_to_formulas`) dirty.
   724	    /// Today this reverse index is populated only by
   725	    /// `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef` — see GAP-R-07 for the scalar-
   726	    /// NameRef precision gap (PlanCache `name_gen` still covers
   727	    /// correctness; per-name precision is the open follow-up).
   728	    ///
   729	    /// Phase 3.10 megaudit H2 (2026-05-12): the prior implementation
   730	    /// only marked DIRECT name-referencing formulas dirty, leaving
   731	    /// downstream-of-downstream stale. Fixed: each directly-affected
   732	    /// formula now seeds a `mark_dirty_from_cell_write` BFS at its
   733	    /// own cell address, fanning out the same way cell writes do.
   734	    pub fn on_set_name(&mut self, name: &str) {
   735	        self.hook_counts.set_name = self.hook_counts.set_name.saturating_add(1);
   736	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   737	        let dependents: Vec<NodeId> = self
   738	            .name_to_formulas
   739	            .get(upper.as_str())
   740	            .map(|s| s.iter().copied().collect())
   741	            .unwrap_or_default();
   742	        for n in dependents {
   743	            self.dirty.insert(n);
   744	            // Phase 3.10 H2 fix: transitive fanout. Whatever depends on
   745	            // this name-referencing formula must also recompute when
   746	            // the name's target changes, just like for a direct cell
   747	            // edit. mark_dirty_from_cell_write does the BFS via the
   748	            // reverse-dep + stripe path so any chain or range dep is
   749	            // reached.
   750	            if let Some((s, r, c)) = self.cell_address_for(n) {
   751	                self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(s, r, c);
   752	            }
   753	        }
   754	    }
   755	
   756	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 5/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   757	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::add_sheet`. Phase 3.1 stub still: bumps
   758	    /// counter. No dirty effect — a new sheet contains no formulas
   759	    /// yet. Phase 4.6 (cross-sheet references) will use this to update
   760	    /// the graph's sheet-id tracking.
