   760	    /// the graph's sheet-id tracking.
   761	    pub fn on_add_sheet(&mut self, _new_sheet: SheetId) {
   762	        self.hook_counts.add_sheet = self.hook_counts.add_sheet.saturating_add(1);
   763	    }
   764	
   765	    /// Phase 3.3 (single-hop) + Phase 3.4 (BFS): internal fanout —
   766	    /// for a write at `(sheet, row, col)`, mark every formula node
   767	    /// that TRANSITIVELY depends on this cell dirty. Two reverse-
   768	    /// direction lookups per visited node:
   769	    ///
   770	    /// 1. `Graph::dependents_for_cell` returns range-dep candidates,
   771	    ///    already filtered by the precision check (DIR-3-02).
   772	    /// 2. `cell_to_formulas[(s,r,c)]` returns direct-cell-dep
   773	    ///    formulas.
   774	    ///
   775	    /// Phase 3.4 added the BFS pass: for each newly-marked formula
   776	    /// `F`, look up `F`'s cell address and recurse. A chain
   777	    /// `=A1 → =B1=A1+1 → =C1=B1+1` thus marks both B1 AND C1 dirty
   778	    /// from a single edit at A1. The `dirty.insert(node)` check
   779	    /// returns false on duplicate insert → BFS visits each node at
   780	    /// most once → cycles in the dep graph are safe.
   781	    fn mark_dirty_from_cell_write(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   782	        use std::collections::VecDeque;
   783	        let mut queue: VecDeque<NodeId> = VecDeque::new();
   784	
   785	        // Phase 3.6 (AGG-3-02): invalidate any cached aggregate whose
   786	        // range contains this cell. Done BEFORE the dirty fanout so a
   787	        // formula whose recompute would otherwise hit a stale cache
   788	        // sees a fresh miss + recomputation. Precision is exact —
   789	        // writes outside every cached range are O(cache_size) with no
   790	        // entry removal.
   791	        self.aggregate_cache.invalidate_at(sheet, row, col);
   792	
   793	        // Seed with the direct fanout from the edited cell (which may
   794	        // not itself be a formula).
   795	        for dep in self.graph.dependents_for_cell(sheet, row, col) {
   796	            if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   797	                queue.push_back(dep);
   798	            }
   799	        }
   800	        if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get(&(sheet, row, col)) {
   801	            for &dep in set {
   802	                if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   803	                    queue.push_back(dep);
   804	                }
   805	            }
   806	        }
   807	
   808	        // BFS: for every newly-marked formula, find what depends on
   809	        // ITS cell and propagate. This is the transitive closure of
   810	        // the reverse-dep relation, scoped to formula nodes.
   811	        while let Some(node) = queue.pop_front() {
   812	            let Some((ns, nr, nc)) = self.cell_address_for(node) else {
   813	                continue;
   814	            };
   815	            for dep in self.graph.dependents_for_cell(ns, nr, nc) {
   816	                if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   817	                    queue.push_back(dep);
   818	                }
   819	            }
   820	            if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get(&(ns, nr, nc)) {
   821	                let snapshot: Vec<NodeId> = set.iter().copied().collect();
   822	                for dep in snapshot {
   823	                    if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   824	                        queue.push_back(dep);
   825	                    }
   826	                }
   827	            }
   828	        }
   829	    }
   830	
   831	    /// Phase 3.3: read-only view of the dirty set. Caller may iterate
   832	    /// to schedule recompute. Use `take_dirty` to atomically claim +
   833	    /// clear.
   834	    pub fn dirty_formulas(&self) -> &HashSet<NodeId> {
   835	        &self.dirty
   836	    }
   837	
   838	    /// Phase 3.3: is this formula currently queued for recompute?
   839	    pub fn is_dirty(&self, node: NodeId) -> bool {
   840	        self.dirty.contains(&node)
   841	    }
   842	
   843	    /// Phase 3.3: claim + clear the dirty set in one move. The Phase
   844	    /// 3.4 Tarjan SCC scheduler will call this once per recompute
   845	    /// cycle. Returning `HashSet<NodeId>` rather than a slice lets
   846	    /// callers re-sort / topologically order without re-allocating.
   847	    pub fn take_dirty(&mut self) -> HashSet<NodeId> {
   848	        std::mem::take(&mut self.dirty)
   849	    }
   850	
   851	    /// **Phase 3.7 (2026-05-12) — VOL-3-01/02 entry point.** Mark every
   852	    /// volatile formula (NOW / RAND / TODAY / RANDBETWEEN / RANDARRAY
   853	    /// / INDIRECT / OFFSET / INFO / CELL, per `is_volatile_function`)
   854	    /// dirty AND fan out the transitive reverse-dep graph from each
   855	    /// volatile cell so downstream formulas recompute too. This is
   856	    /// the "F9 / explicit recalc" trigger.
   857	    ///
   858	    /// Returns the number of volatile formulas marked. Zero means no
   859	    /// volatile formulas exist in the workbook — `recompute_dirty`
   860	    /// after this call will be a no-op.
   861	    pub fn mark_volatile_dirty(&mut self) -> usize {
   862	        let volatile: Vec<NodeId> = self.volatile_formulas.iter().copied().collect();
   863	        let count = volatile.len();
   864	        for v in volatile {
   865	            self.dirty.insert(v);
   866	            // Fan out from the volatile cell's address so downstream
   867	            // formulas reading its value also recompute (VOL-3-02).
   868	            // `mark_dirty_from_cell_write` does BFS via the reverse-
   869	            // dep + stripe path, so any chain or range dep is reached.
   870	            if let Some((s, r, c)) = self.cell_address_for(v) {
   871	                self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(s, r, c);
   872	            }
   873	        }
   874	        count
   875	    }

   877	    /// Phase 3.7: count of formulas currently marked volatile (Phase
   878	    /// 3.2 populates via `is_volatile_function`). Test-only
   879	    /// observability; product code consumes `volatile_formulas()` or
   880	    /// calls `mark_volatile_dirty()` directly.
   881	    pub fn volatile_count(&self) -> usize {
   882	        self.volatile_formulas.len()
   883	    }

   889	    pub fn aggregate_cache(&self) -> &InMemAggregateCache {
   890	        &self.aggregate_cache

   904	    /// live on the Graph's stripe index — query

   910	    /// Phase 3.4: address lookup for a formula node. The graph's
   911	    /// `Node::Cell` payload carries `(sheet, row, col)` — this is the
   912	    /// inverse of `cell_node_for`. Returns `None` if the node is not
   913	    /// a Cell variant (today every NodeId in `cell_index` is a Cell;
   914	    /// Phase 4 may add Range/Region nodes that aren't formula cells).
   915	    pub fn cell_address_for(&self, node: NodeId) -> Option<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> {
   916	        match self.graph.node(node) {
   917	            Node::Cell(CellNode { sheet, row, col }) => Some((*sheet, *row, *col)),
   918	            _ => None,
   919	        }
   920	    }
   921	
   922	    /// **Phase 3.4 acceptance (SCH-3-01..04 entry point).** Atomically
   923	    /// claim the dirty set and run the Phase 0 W3-3 iterative Tarjan
   924	    /// scheduler over it. The returned [`Schedule`] partitions nodes:
   925	    ///
   926	    /// - `sorted`: dependency-first topological order. Evaluate
   927	    ///   `sorted[0]` before `sorted[1]`, etc.
   928	    /// - `cycled`: nodes in a non-trivial strongly connected component
   929	    ///   (size > 1) or with a self-loop. Per Phase 0 spec these get
   930	    ///   `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ)`.
   931	    ///
   932	    /// The dirty set is cleared by this call. Edges to non-dirty
   933	    /// nodes are not traversed (those nodes aren't being recomputed;
   934	    /// their current values are read as-is). Input ordering is
   935	    /// sorted-by-NodeId before the scheduler runs so SCH-3-03
   936	    /// determinism holds: same workbook + same edits → same schedule
   937	    /// across runs (the underlying scheduler is already deterministic
   938	    /// in its input slice order; we just normalize HashSet iteration).
   939	    pub fn schedule_dirty(&mut self) -> Schedule {
   940	        let mut dirty_vec: Vec<NodeId> = self.dirty.drain().collect();
   941	        dirty_vec.sort();
   942	        schedule(&self.graph, &dirty_vec)
   943	    }
   944	}

   952	    fn workbook_with_formulas(formulas: &[(SheetId, RowId, ColId, &str)]) -> Workbook {
   953	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
   954	        // Ensure enough sheets exist.
   955	        let max_sheet = formulas.iter().map(|(s, _, _, _)| *s).max().unwrap_or(0);
   956	        for i in 0..=max_sheet {
   957	            wb.add_sheet(format!("S{i}"));
   958	        }
   959	        for (sheet, row, col, text) in formulas {
   960	            wb.put_at(*sheet, *row, *col, Value::Blank);
   961	            wb.put_formula(*sheet, *row, *col, *text);
   962	        }
   963	        wb
   964	    }

  1001	    /// G3-01: deterministic rebuild — same workbook → same node IDs in
  1002	    /// the same order across runs. Without sorting, Workbook's HashMap
  1003	    /// iteration order would give different `NodeId`s on different
  1004	    /// runs.
  1005	    #[test]
  1006	    fn rebuild_is_deterministic_across_runs() {
  1007	        let wb = workbook_with_formulas(&[
  1008	            (0, 7, 7, "100"),
  1009	            (0, 0, 0, "1 + 1"),
  1010	            (1, 3, 2, "A1 + 1"),
  1011	            (0, 5, 5, "5 + 5"),
  1012	        ]);
  1013	        let mut prior_ids: Option<Vec<NodeId>> = None;
  1014	        for _ in 0..10 {
  1015	            let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  1016	            assert!(r.is_complete());
  1017	            let s = r.session;
  1018	            // Collect node ids in sheet/row/col-sorted order.
  1019	            let mut ids: Vec<((SheetId, RowId, ColId), NodeId)> =
  1020	                s.cell_index.iter().map(|(k, v)| (*k, *v)).collect();
  1021	            ids.sort_by_key(|(k, _)| *k);
  1022	            let just_ids: Vec<NodeId> = ids.iter().map(|(_, id)| *id).collect();
  1023	            if let Some(prev) = prior_ids.as_ref() {
  1024	                assert_eq!(*prev, just_ids, "rebuild produced different node IDs");
  1025	            }
  1026	            prior_ids = Some(just_ids);
  1027	        }
  1028	    }

  3010	    /// Phase 3.4: with no graph attached, `recompute_dirty` returns
  3011	    /// None — the dirty set lives on the session, so without one

  3016	        let reg = default_registry();

  3021	    /// SCH-3-01 end-to-end: edit a chain head, recompute_dirty
  3022	    /// cascades through the chain. A1=1; B1=A1+1; C1=B1+1. After
  3023	    /// set_value(A1, 10), B1 and C1 should both update to 11 and 12.
  3024	    #[test]
  3025	    fn recompute_dirty_cascades_dependency_chain() {
  3026	        use crate::CalcgraphSession;
  3027	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
  3028	        let reg = default_registry();
  3029	        let mut graph = CalcgraphSession::new();
  3030	        // Seed via the runtime so the graph gets the hooks.
  3031	        {
  3032	            let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_graph(&mut wb, &reg, &mut graph);
  3033	            rt.set_value(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(1.0)).unwrap();
  3034	            rt.set_formula(0, 0, 1, "A1 + 1").unwrap();
  3035	            rt.set_formula(0, 0, 2, "B1 + 1").unwrap();
  3036	        }
  3037	        // After the initial sets, B1 = 2, C1 = 3.
  3038	        assert_eq!(wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 1)), Value::Number(2.0));
  3039	        assert_eq!(wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 2)), Value::Number(3.0));
  3040	
  3041	        // Edit A1 → 10 — the chain must propagate.
  3042	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_graph(&mut wb, &reg, &mut graph);
  3043	        rt.set_value(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(10.0)).unwrap();
  3044	        let result = rt.recompute_dirty().expect("graph attached");
  3045	        // Both B1 and C1 were dirty; both recomputed cleanly.
  3046	        assert_eq!(result.attempted, 2);
  3047	        assert_eq!(result.succeeded, 2);
  3048	        assert!(result.failures.is_empty());
  3049	
  3050	        // Verify: B1 = 11, C1 = 12.
  3051	        assert_eq!(
  3052	            wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 1)),
  3053	            Value::Number(11.0),
  3054	            "B1 = A1 + 1 = 11"
  3055	        );
  3056	        assert_eq!(
  3057	            wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 2)),
  3058	            Value::Number(12.0),
  3059	            "C1 = B1 + 1 = 12"
  3060	        );
  3061	    }
  3062	
  3063	    /// SCH-3-02 end-to-end: cycles get `#CIRC!`. Build A1 = B1 + 1
  3064	    /// and B1 = A1 + 1 (a 2-cycle), then trigger a recompute. Every
  3065	    /// cell in the SCC must be `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ)`.
  3066	    ///
  3067	    /// Note: we build the cycle directly via the workbook's low-level
  3068	    /// `put_formula` API and rebuild the session. Going through
  3069	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula` for the second cycle member
  3070	    /// would re-evaluate A1 mid-cycle and write a non-cycle value,
  3071	    /// which is fine — but it complicates the test setup. The
  3072	    /// rebuild path is the canonical "load existing workbook" entry
  3073	    /// the IDE uses and is the cleanest way to set up the test.
  3074	    #[test]
  3075	    fn recompute_dirty_writes_circ_error_for_cycle_members() {
  3076	        use crate::CalcgraphSession;
  3077	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
  3078	        let reg = default_registry();
  3079	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Blank);
  3080	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Blank);
  3081	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 0, "B1 + 1");
  3082	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 1, "A1 + 1");
  3083	        let rebuild = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  3084	        assert!(rebuild.is_complete());
  3085	        let mut graph = rebuild.session;
  3086	        assert!(graph.dirty_formulas().is_empty(), "rebuild starts clean");
  3087	
  3088	        // Mark the cycle dirty. on_set_value(A1) → cell_to_formulas[A1]
  3089	        // = {B1} → mark B1 dirty. BFS from B1 → cell_to_formulas[B1]
  3090	        // = {A1} → mark A1 dirty. The Tarjan SCC scheduler discovers
  3091	        // the cycle.
  3092	        graph.on_set_value(0, 0, 0);
  3093	        assert_eq!(graph.dirty_formulas().len(), 2);
  3094	
  3095	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_graph(&mut wb, &reg, &mut graph);
  3096	        let result = rt.recompute_dirty().expect("graph attached");
  3097	        // Both members are in cycled (not sorted); attempted == 2.
  3098	        assert_eq!(result.attempted, 2);
  3099	        // `succeeded` counts only sorted-path evaluations.
  3100	        assert_eq!(result.succeeded, 0);
  3101	        assert!(result.failures.is_empty());
  3102	
  3103	        assert_eq!(
  3104	            wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 0)),
  3105	            Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ),
  3106	            "A1 in cycle → #CIRC!"
  3107	        );
  3108	        assert_eq!(
  3109	            wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 1)),
  3110	            Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ),
  3111	            "B1 in cycle → #CIRC!"
  3112	        );
  3113	    }

   866	    /// **Engine Phase 3.4 (2026-05-12) — W5-37 SCH-3-01..04 entry
   867	    /// point.** Recompute only the formulas the attached
   868	    /// [`CalcgraphSession`] has marked dirty since the last call.
   869	    /// The scheduler runs iterative Tarjan SCC over the dirty
   870	    /// subset; nodes in non-trivial SCCs (or with self-loops) are
   871	    /// written as `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ)`. Everything else
   872	    /// is evaluated in topological order via the existing PlanCache
   873	    /// pipeline.
   874	    ///
   875	    /// Returns `None` when no `CalcgraphSession` is attached (the
   876	    /// runtime was constructed via `new` or `with_oplog`); the
   877	    /// dirty-set lives on the session, so we can't do incremental
   878	    /// recompute without it. Callers in that mode should use
   879	    /// `recompute_all` for a full HashMap-order pass instead.
   880	    ///
   881	    /// Returns `Some(RecomputeResult)` otherwise, matching the
   882	    /// aggregation shape of `recompute_all`: per-cell failures
   883	    /// (parse / bind issues that have somehow surfaced post-bind,
   884	    /// e.g. a name was deleted between extract and recompute) are
   885	    /// collected, the rest succeed.
   886	    pub fn recompute_dirty(&mut self) -> Option<RecomputeResult> {
   887	        // The session lives behind an `Option<&'a mut CalcgraphSession>`
   888	        // — take it out for the duration of this call so we can borrow
   889	        // both the workbook and the session simultaneously. Reattach
   890	        // before returning.
   891	        let mut session_slot = self.graph.take();
   892	        let session = session_slot.as_deref_mut()?;
   893	
   894	        // Phase 3.4: claim dirty + topo-sort. Edges in the graph
   895	        // model the dep direction (`outgoing(F) = what F depends on`).
   896	        // Tarjan emits SCCs in reverse-topo of the condensation
   897	        // which, for our edge orientation, is dependency-first order.
   898	        let sched = session.schedule_dirty();
   899	        let attempted = sched.total_count();
   900	        let mut succeeded = 0;
   901	        let mut failures: Vec<RecomputeFailure> = Vec::new();
   902	        let mut skipped_value_equality: usize = 0;
   903	        // Phase 3.9 (W5-42): SIMD-eligibility profile. Incremented
   904	        // each time a re-evaluated formula's plan classifies into a
   905	        // recognized `SimdShape` (i.e. not `NotApplicable`). V1
   906	        // observability only — actual bulk SIMD dispatch via
   907	        // `simd::*` happens on the bench / FormulaRegion path
   908	        // (Phase 4.7+). The fact that the graph scheduler counts
   909	        // these tells the IDE / profile reader where region
   910	        // optimization would pay off.
   911	        let mut simd_classified: usize = 0;
   912	
   913	        // Phase 3.8 (W5-41, VEQ-3-01..03) — value-equality short-
   914	        // circuit. State for the pass:
   915	        //   - `prior`: workbook value at each sched node BEFORE we
   916	        //     touch anything. Snapshotted once, used as the equality
   917	        //     baseline.
   918	        //   - `originally_dirty`: NodeIds that were in the dirty set
   919	        //     coming in to this call. Used to distinguish "top-level
   920	        //     dirty" (came from an external edit; must re-eval) from
   921	        //     "downstream dirty" (only here because an upstream
   922	        //     formula was dirty; check if upstream actually changed).
   923	        //   - `changed`: addresses whose value differs from `prior`
   924	        //     after this pass. Built as we go; downstream formulas
   925	        //     check membership to decide whether to re-eval.
   926	        use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
   927	        let mut prior: HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), Value> = HashMap::new();
   928	        let mut originally_dirty: HashSet<ql_calcgraph::NodeId> = HashSet::new();
   929	        for n in sched.sorted.iter().chain(sched.cycled.iter()) {
   930	            originally_dirty.insert(*n);
   931	            if let Some(addr) = session.cell_address_for(*n) {
   932	                prior.insert(
   933	                    addr,
   934	                    self.workbook
   935	                        .read(ql_types::Address::new(addr.0, addr.1, addr.2)),
   936	                );
   937	            }
   938	        }
   939	        let mut changed: HashSet<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = HashSet::new();
   940	

 succeeded in 331ms:
  1700	        wb.add_sheet("S0");
  1701	        wb.set_name(
  1702	            "ColA",
  1703	            ql_storage::NamedTarget::Range(Range {
  1704	                sheet: 0,
  1705	                start_row: 0,
  1706	                end_row: RowId::MAX,
  1707	                start_col: 0,
  1708	                end_col: 0,
  1709	            }),
  1710	        )
  1711	        .unwrap();
  1712	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Blank);
  1713	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 1, "SUM(ColA)");
  1714	
  1715	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  1716	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  1717	        let s = r.session;
  1718	
  1719	        // Exactly one stripe entry (Column 0 on Sheet 0).
  1720	        assert_eq!(
  1721	            s.graph().stripe_index().stripe_count(),
  1722	            1,
  1723	            "WholeColumn must register exactly one Column stripe"
  1724	        );
  1725	        // Zero per-cell edges — the formula's deps live on the stripe
  1726	        // map + formula_to_range_deps, not as Graph edges.
  1727	        assert_eq!(
  1728	            s.graph().edge_count(),
  1729	            0,
  1730	            "no per-cell edges for whole-column named range"
  1731	        );
  1732	        // The formula's range registration count is 1.
  1733	        assert_eq!(s.graph().range_dependency_count(), 1);

  1809	    /// `on_clear_formula` removes the cleared formula from the dirty
  1810	    /// set AND propagates dirty to its downstream dependents.
  1811	    #[test]
  1812	    fn clear_formula_evicts_self_and_dirties_downstream() {
  1813	        // C1 = A1 + B1; D1 = C1 + 1. Clearing C1 dirties D1 but not C1.
  1814	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  1815	        wb.add_sheet("S0");
  1816	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(1.0));
  1817	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Number(2.0));
  1818	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 2, Value::Blank);
  1819	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 3, Value::Blank);
  1820	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 2, "A1 + B1");
  1821	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 3, "C1 + 1");
  1822	
  1823	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  1824	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  1825	        let mut s = r.session;
  1826	        let c1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 2).unwrap();
  1827	        let d1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 3).unwrap();
  1828	
  1829	        // Pre-mark C1 dirty (simulating a prior pending recompute).
  1830	        s.dirty.insert(c1);
  1831	        s.dirty.insert(d1);
  1832	
  1833	        s.on_clear_formula(0, 0, 2);
  1834	        // C1 evicted (it no longer computes).
  1835	        assert!(!s.is_dirty(c1));
  1836	        // D1 still in dirty (we re-marked it via mark_dirty_from_cell_write
  1837	        // since the C1 value changed).
  1838	        assert!(s.is_dirty(d1));
  1839	    }

  1841	    /// Phase 3.3 + Phase 3.2 interplay: re-binding a formula evicts
  1842	    /// stale cell deps from `cell_to_formulas`, so writes to old deps
  1843	    /// no longer fire dirty marks.
  1844	    #[test]
  1845	    fn rebind_clears_cell_reverse_index_for_old_deps() {
  1846	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  1847	        // Plan v1: =A1 — depends on A1 only.
  1848	        let plan_v1 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  1849	            sheet: 0,
  1850	            row: 0,
  1851	            col: 0,
  1852	            abs_col: false,
  1853	            abs_row: false,
  1854	        };
  1855	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v1);
  1856	        let node = s.cell_node_for(0, 5, 5).unwrap();
  1857	        assert_eq!(s.cell_dep_count(), 1, "A1 indexed");
  1858	
  1859	        // Plan v2: =B1 — depends on B1 only. A1 reverse-index entry
  1860	        // must be evicted by `remove_formula_deps`.
  1861	        let plan_v2 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  1862	            sheet: 0,
  1863	            row: 0,
  1864	            col: 1,
  1865	            abs_col: false,
  1866	            abs_row: false,
  1867	        };
  1868	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v2);
  1869	        assert_eq!(s.cell_dep_count(), 1, "only B1 now");
  1870	
  1871	        // Take dirty (clear residue from the two on_set_formula calls).
  1872	        s.take_dirty();
  1873	
  1874	        // Write to A1 — formula must NOT dirty (no longer a dep).
  1875	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 0);

  1877	            !s.is_dirty(node),
  1878	            "stale A1 dep should not fire after re-bind"
  1879	        );
  1880	
  1881	        // Write to B1 — fires.
  1882	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 1);
  1883	        assert!(s.is_dirty(node));

  1915	    // Phase 3.4 acceptance gates (SCH-3-01..04).
  1916	    //
  1917	    // The Phase 0 W3-3 iterative Tarjan scheduler already exists in
  1918	    // ql-calcgraph::topo. Phase 3.4 wires it: extract_and_register_deps
  1919	    // adds forward edges for direct cell→cell deps so Tarjan can
  1920	    // discover the dep graph; schedule_dirty consumes the session's
  1921	    // dirty set and returns (sorted, cycled).
  1922	    //
  1923	    // These tests live at the session level — they verify the
  1924	    // schedule's shape directly. The matching end-to-end recompute
  1925	    // tests live in `workbook_runtime.rs` (where the runtime evaluates
  1926	    // the schedule's `sorted` order and writes `#CIRC!` for the
  1927	    // `cycled` set).

  1930	    /// Helper: workbook for the canonical dependency-chain test.
  1931	    /// A1 = 1 (literal); B1 = A1 + 1; C1 = B1 + 1.
  1932	    fn chain_workbook() -> Workbook {
  1933	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  1934	        wb.add_sheet("S0");
  1935	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(1.0));
  1936	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Blank);
  1937	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 2, Value::Blank);
  1938	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 1, "A1 + 1");
  1939	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 2, "B1 + 1");
  1940	        wb
  1941	    }
  1942	
  1943	    /// SCH-3-01: dependency chains schedule in correct order. With
  1944	    /// A1=literal, B1=A1+1, C1=B1+1, marking A1 dirty propagates to
  1945	    /// B1 + C1 (via cell_to_formulas) and the schedule orders
  1946	    /// B1 BEFORE C1 (because C1 depends on B1).
  1947	    #[test]
  1948	    fn sch_3_01_dependency_chains_schedule_in_dependency_first_order() {
  1949	        let wb = chain_workbook();
  1950	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  1951	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  1952	        let mut s = r.session;
  1953	        let b1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).unwrap();
  1954	        let c1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 2).unwrap();
  1955	
  1956	        // Phase 3.4 invariant: the forward edge from C1 to B1 exists
  1957	        // in the graph (extract_and_register_deps added it on the
  1958	        // rebuild pre-pass + extract).
  1959	        assert!(
  1960	            s.graph().outgoing(c1).contains(&b1),
  1961	            "C1 must have an outgoing edge to B1 (the dep)"
  1962	        );
  1963	
  1964	        // Edit A1 — both B1 and C1 dirty.
  1965	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 0);
  1966	        assert!(s.is_dirty(b1));
  1967	        assert!(s.is_dirty(c1));
  1968	
  1969	        let sched = s.schedule_dirty();
  1970	        assert!(sched.cycled.is_empty(), "no cycles in a linear chain");
  1971	        // B1 must come before C1 in sorted order.
  1972	        let pos_b1 = sched.sorted.iter().position(|n| *n == b1).unwrap();
  1973	        let pos_c1 = sched.sorted.iter().position(|n| *n == c1).unwrap();
  1974	        assert!(
  1975	            pos_b1 < pos_c1,
  1976	            "B1 must schedule before C1 (B1 is C1's dep)"
  1977	        );
  1978	
  1979	        // dirty set is cleared after schedule_dirty.
  1980	        assert!(s.dirty_formulas().is_empty());
  1981	    }
  1982	
  1983	    /// SCH-3-02: cycles surface in `Schedule::cycled` carrying every
  1984	    /// SCC member. Build a 2-cycle (A1 = B1 + 1; B1 = A1 + 1) and a
  1985	    /// self-loop (D1 = D1 + 1) and verify the cycle reporter sees
  1986	    /// every involved node.
  1987	    #[test]
  1988	    fn sch_3_02_cycles_report_all_scc_members() {
  1989	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  1990	        wb.add_sheet("S0");
  1991	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Blank);
  1992	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Blank);
  1993	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 3, Value::Blank);
  1994	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 0, "B1 + 1");
  1995	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 1, "A1 + 1");
  1996	        // Self-cycle at D1.
  1997	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 3, "D1 + 1");
  1998	
  1999	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  2000	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  2001	        let mut s = r.session;
  2002	        let a1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 0).unwrap();
  2003	        let b1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).unwrap();
  2004	        let d1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 3).unwrap();
  2005	
  2006	        // Force the cycle into dirty: edit any of A1/B1, the dirty
  2007	        // walk picks up the cycle members via direct-cell-dep reverse
  2008	        // index. We seed both formulas + the self-loop directly.
  2009	        s.dirty.insert(a1);
  2010	        s.dirty.insert(b1);
  2011	        s.dirty.insert(d1);
  2012	
  2013	        let sched = s.schedule_dirty();
  2014	        assert!(
  2015	            sched.cycled.contains(&a1),
  2016	            "A1 must surface in cycled — part of the 2-cycle"
  2017	        );
  2018	        assert!(
  2019	            sched.cycled.contains(&b1),
  2020	            "B1 must surface in cycled — part of the 2-cycle"
  2021	        );
  2022	        assert!(
  2023	            sched.cycled.contains(&d1),
  2024	            "D1 must surface in cycled — self-loop"
  2025	        );
  2026	        // Sorted should be empty in this case (every dirty node is in
  2027	        // a cycle).
  2028	        assert!(sched.sorted.is_empty());
  2029	    }

  2031	    /// SCH-3-03: schedule is deterministic across runs. Two
  2032	    /// independent rebuilds of the same workbook + same edit sequence
  2033	    /// produce the same Schedule.
  2034	    #[test]
  2035	    fn sch_3_03_schedule_is_deterministic_across_runs() {
  2036	        fn run() -> (Vec<NodeId>, Vec<NodeId>) {
  2037	            let wb = chain_workbook();
  2038	            let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  2039	            let mut s = r.session;
  2040	            s.on_set_value(0, 0, 0); // edit A1
  2041	            let sched = s.schedule_dirty();
  2042	            (sched.sorted, sched.cycled)
  2043	        }
  2044	        let r1 = run();
  2045	        let r2 = run();
  2046	        assert_eq!(r1, r2, "schedule must be byte-identical across runs");

  2049	    /// SCH-3-04: schedule operates ONLY on the dirty subset. An
  2050	    /// edit to A1 dirties B1 + C1 but does NOT include unrelated
  2051	    /// formula E1 (which references some other cell).
  2052	    #[test]
  2053	    fn sch_3_04_dirty_subset_avoids_unrelated_formulas() {
  2054	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  2055	        wb.add_sheet("S0");
  2056	        // A1=lit, B1=A1+1, C1=B1+1 — the chain.
  2057	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(1.0));
  2058	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Blank);
  2059	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 2, Value::Blank);
  2060	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 1, "A1 + 1");
  2061	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 2, "B1 + 1");
  2062	        // E1 = Z1 + 1 — unrelated to A1/B1/C1.
  2063	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 4, Value::Blank);
  2064	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 25, Value::Number(7.0));
  2065	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 4, "Z1 + 1");
  2066	
  2067	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  2068	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  2069	        let mut s = r.session;
  2070	        let b1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).unwrap();
  2071	        let c1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 2).unwrap();
  2072	        let e1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 4).unwrap();
  2073	
  2074	        // Edit A1 only.
  2075	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 0);
  2076	        let sched = s.schedule_dirty();
  2077	        let all: HashSet<NodeId> = sched
  2078	            .sorted
  2079	            .iter()
  2080	            .chain(sched.cycled.iter())
  2081	            .copied()

   941	        // Cycled nodes get `#CIRC!` regardless of whether the formula
   942	        // still binds. Spec: cycles are terminal Phase 0 errors. Phase
   943	        // 3.5: `#CIRC!` is a FORMULA-output value (the formula's
   944	        // resolution), so it routes to the computed overlay. Phase
   945	        // 3.8: still apply value-equality (the cycle might have been
   946	        // pre-existing → `#CIRC!` matches prior `#CIRC!`; skip write).
   947	        let circ = Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ);
   948	        for node in &sched.cycled {
   949	            let Some((sheet, row, col)) = session.cell_address_for(*node) else {
   950	                continue;
   951	            };
   952	            let prior_val = prior.get(&(sheet, row, col));
   953	            if prior_val == Some(&circ) {
   954	                skipped_value_equality += 1;
   955	            } else {
   956	                self.workbook.put_computed_at(sheet, row, col, circ.clone());
   957	                changed.insert((sheet, row, col));
   958	            }
   959	        }
   960	
   961	        // Sorted nodes evaluate in dependency-first order.
   962	        for node in &sched.sorted {
   963	            let Some((sheet, row, col)) = session.cell_address_for(*node) else {
   964	                continue;
   965	            };
   966	            let Some(text) = self.workbook.formula_at(sheet, row, col).cloned() else {
   967	                continue;
   968	            };
   969	
   970	            // Phase 3.8: VEQ decision — does any upstream of `node`
   971	            // have a reason to recompute? Cases:
   972	            //   a) Volatile (NOW/RAND/etc.) — always re-eval (its
   973	            //      value can change without any cell edit).
   974	            //   b) Has named-range deps — V1 conservatively re-evals
   975	            //      (we don't track per-cell-in-range changes yet).
   976	            //   c) Has at least one direct-cell dep that's a
   977	            //      formula in the original dirty set AND that
   978	            //      formula's value changed → re-eval.
   979	            //   d) Has NO direct-cell dep that's in the original
   980	            //      dirty set (top-level dirty; came from an external
   981	            //      edit) → re-eval.
   982	            //   e) All direct-cell deps are in the dirty set but
   983	            //      none ended up in `changed` → SKIP.
   984	            let is_volatile = session.is_volatile(*node);
   985	            let needs_eval = if is_volatile {
   986	                true
   987	            } else if let Some(deps) = session.formula_deps(*node) {
   988	                if !deps.named_ranges.is_empty() {
   989	                    true
   990	                } else {
   991	                    let mut had_dirty_dep = false;
   992	                    let mut had_changed_dep = false;
   993	                    for &(ds, dr, dc) in &deps.cells {
   994	                        if let Some(dep_node) = session.cell_node_for(ds, dr, dc) {
   995	                            if originally_dirty.contains(&dep_node) {
   996	                                had_dirty_dep = true;
   997	                                if changed.contains(&(ds, dr, dc)) {
   998	                                    had_changed_dep = true;
   999	                                    break;
  1000	                                }
  1001	                            }
  1002	                        }
  1003	                    }
  1004	                    // Top-level dirty (no dep in dirty set) OR a
  1005	                    // changed dirty-dep → re-eval. Pure-downstream
  1006	                    // with no changed dep → skip.
  1007	                    !had_dirty_dep || had_changed_dep
  1008	                }
  1009	            } else {
  1010	                // No tracked deps (e.g., `=1+1`). Treat as top-level.
  1011	                true
  1012	            };
  1013	
  1014	            if !needs_eval {
  1015	                skipped_value_equality += 1;
  1016	                continue;
  1017	            }
  1018	
  1019	            // Phase 3.6 (W5-39): route through the session's aggregate
  1020	            // cache so `SUM(Sales)`-style formulas hit the cache when
  1021	            // no cell inside `Sales` changed (AGG-3-01).
  1022	            // Phase 3.9 (W5-42): also classify the plan for SIMD
  1023	            // eligibility (SIMD-3-03 profile). We need the plan
  1024	            // post-bind — call a slightly-expanded helper that
  1025	            // returns the value AND the SimdShape classification.
  1026	            let agg_cache = session.aggregate_cache();
  1027	            match self.try_recompute_with_simd_profile(sheet, &text, agg_cache) {

  1029	                    if simd_eligible {
  1030	                        simd_classified += 1;
  1031	                    }

  1033	                    // computed value matches the snapshot, suppress
  1034	                    // the write entirely and don't record this cell
  1035	                    // as `changed` — downstream formulas that depend
  1036	                    // only on this one will skip too.
  1037	                    let prior_val = prior.get(&(sheet, row, col));

  1040	                    } else {

**Verdict**

I agree with the split framing: **GAP-G-01 and GAP-G-03 are independent correctness failures**, and the surgical path is **Option A + small Option C**, with FormulaRegion deferred to Phase 4.7.

**1. Independence**

Yes. G-01 is stale stored graph/range state after formula rebind. G-03 is missing scheduler visibility for live range deps. Fixing A does not make range deps scheduler edges; fixing C does not revoke stale direct edges or stale stripe/range registrations.

The interaction is mostly positive: Option C must read the **current** `formula_to_range_deps`; Option A makes that map current by clearing stale entries before re-registering. Without A, C can amplify G-01 by scheduling against stale range deps.

**2. Option C Correctness**

Option C is correct if scoped precisely:

- At scheduling time, consider dirty **formula nodes**, not arbitrary literal dirty cells.
- For each dirty formula `F` with range deps, inspect each dirty formula node `G`; if `G`’s cell address is inside one of `F`’s ranges, add temp edge `F -> G`.
- Do not materialize edges to non-formula cells; they are not schedulable nodes and are read as current values.

This matches the existing Tarjan contract in [topo.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/topo.rs:40): edges to non-dirty nodes are intentionally ignored.

Partial dirty sets are acceptable under current semantics. If `A1 = SUM(B1:B1)` is dirty and `B1` is clean, evaluating A1 against current B1 is correct. If B1 should be dirty because of a name/range/formula change, the runtime’s dirty fanout must mark it. The H2 fix in `on_set_name` helps here by marking name users and fanning out from their cells via [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:734).

One caveat: current `schedule_dirty()` drains the dirty set and calls `schedule(&self.graph, ...)` directly at [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:939). Option C either needs a scheduler variant that accepts extra adjacency, or a session-level wrapper that computes temp edges before draining/after collecting `dirty_vec`.

**3. Option A Shape**

Yes, `clear_outgoing(node)` must also remove `node` from each former target’s `incoming[target]`; otherwise dirty propagation and Tarjan invariants diverge. Linear removal is acceptable for the current graph because normal formulas have tiny out-degree. High in-degree targets make removal scan larger, but rebind frequency is user/edit-rate, not recompute hot path.

Implementation notes:

- Add `AdjacencyVectors::clear_outgoing(from) -> Vec<NodeId>` or directly remove back-pointers inside it.
- Increment graph revision once per clear that changes state, not per removed edge unless current observability expects per-edge revision churn.
- Add `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)`.
- Add `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula(node)` backed by `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>`; otherwise full stripe scans are the real scale risk.
- Wire this at the same point as current `remove_formula_deps` in [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:440).

Determinism is fine if dependency extraction order is deterministic. Rebind from the same plan should restore the same outgoing order.

**4. Option D**

Epoch-marked edges are worse here: they keep stale memory and push correctness filtering into every scheduler/dirty traversal. `IndexSet` is plausible but adds a dep and changes memory profile. Rebuilding scheduler edges from plans every recompute is conceptually clean but pays bind/extract cost on the hot path and bypasses the existing session-side live dep model.

Best “D” is a bounded variant of A+C: keep `Vec` adjacency, add revocation APIs, and add temp range adjacency only for dirty formula nodes.

**5. Effort Estimate**

“3-5 days” looks conservative. The core A change is smaller because the session already has the rebind choke point: `extract_and_register_deps` already calls `remove_formula_deps` at [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:449). The work is mostly API, symmetry tests, and regression tests.

I’d estimate:

- Option A: 1 strong session, maybe 2 if graph stats/revision expectations need cleanup.
- Option C: 0.5-1 session.
- Full verification across Phase 3.1-3.8 surfaces is prudent, but not a 3-5 day refactor unless hidden tests depend on append-only duplicate edge behavior.

**6. Stop Conditions**

Defer A+C only if:

- Phase 4.3 V2 actually requires FormulaRegion for function semantics, not just future performance.
- `formula_to_stripe_keys` memory is unacceptable at target scale.
- Clearing reverse incoming edges shows measurable edit-latency regression on high fan-in sheets.
- Option C cannot be made deterministic without awkward scheduler surgery.

What I’d watch during implementation: stale direct-edge false cycles, stale range dirtying after rebind, range-induced cycles, range-induced ordering, self-range cycles, and VEQ behavior for range formulas. The most important regression tests are exactly the H1/H3/H4 examples plus a “same rebind same schedule order” determinism test.

- Clearing reverse incoming edges shows measurable edit-latency regression on high fan-in sheets.
- Option C cannot be made deterministic without awkward scheduler surgery.

What I’d watch during implementation: stale direct-edge false cycles, stale range dirtying after rebind, range-induced cycles, range-induced ordering, self-range cycles, and VEQ behavior for range formulas. The most important regression tests are exactly the H1/H3/H4 examples plus a “same rebind same schedule order” determinism test.
_row] inclusive.
            let chunk_rows = self.chunk_rows;
            let key = ChunkKey {
                sheet,
                col,
                chunk_idx,
            };
            let bits = self.chunks.entry(key).or_insert_with(|| {
                let words = (chunk_rows as usize).div_ceil(64);
                vec![0u64; words]
            });
            let bit_lo = (row_lo - chunk_first_row) as usize;
            let bit_hi = (row_hi - chunk_first_row) as usize;
            for bit in bit_lo..=bit_hi {
                bits[bit / 64] |= 1u64 << (bit % 64);
            }
        }
    }

    /// Test whether a specific cell is marked dirty.
    pub fn is_cell_dirty(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> bool {
        let chunk_idx = row / self.chunk_rows;
        let in_chunk_row = (row % self.chunk_rows) as usize;
        let key = ChunkKey {
            sheet,
            col,
            chunk_idx,
        };
        match self.chunks.get(&key) {
            Some(bits) => (bits[in_chunk_row / 64] >> (in_chunk_row % 64)) & 1 == 1,
            None => false,
        }
    }
}

/// Walk the reverse adjacency from each seed, marking dependents' chunks dirty. Each node
/// is visited at most once even in the presence of cycles (a defensive precaution; W3-3
/// `topo::schedule` partitions cycled nodes into `Schedule::cycled` for the evaluator to
/// surface as `CellError(Cycle)`).
///
/// Seeds are the nodes that just changed. Semantics by seed type:
/// - `Node::Cell` — marks exactly the cell's chunk bit dirty.
/// - `Node::FormulaRegion` — marks every cell in the region's `target_rect` dirty.
/// - `Node::Range` — no-op for chunk marking (range nodes don't own chunks); the BFS
///   still walks through them to find downstream formula dependents.
/// - `Node::Spill` — panics; Phase 3+ feature, must not appear in Phase 0.
///
/// In practice callers seed with `CellNode`s (the binder feeds cell writes). The
/// FormulaRegion/Range/Spill behavior is documented for completeness; no Phase 0 code
/// path uses non-Cell seeds.
pub fn propagate_from_cells(graph: &Graph, dirty: &mut ChunkDirtySet, seeds: &[NodeId]) {
    let mut visited: HashSet<NodeId> = HashSet::new();
    let mut queue: VecDeque<NodeId> = VecDeque::new();

    for &id in seeds {
        if visited.insert(id) {
            mark_node_chunks(graph, dirty, id);
            queue.push_back(id);
        }
    }

    while let Some(node_id) = queue.pop_front() {
        for &dependent in graph.incoming(node_id) {
            if visited.insert(dependent) {
                mark_node_chunks(graph, dirty, dependent);
                queue.push_back(dependent);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn mark_node_chunks(graph: &Graph, dirty: &mut ChunkDirtySet, node_id: NodeId) {
    match graph.node(node_id) {
        Node::Cell(c) => dirty.mark_cell(c.sheet, c.row, c.col),
        Node::FormulaRegion(frn) => {
            // The whole target rectangle recomputes; mark every cell.
            for col in frn.target_start_col..=frn.target_end_col {
                dirty.mark_range_in_column(
                    frn.sheet,
                    frn.target_start_row,
                    frn.target_end_row,
                    col,
                );
            }
        }
        Node::Range(_) => {
            // Range nodes don't own chunks — they're edge intermediaries from formula
            // vertices to source cells. The walk continues through them (the queue+visited
            // mechanism in propagate_from_cells handles that). No chunks to mark here.
        }
        Node::Spill(_) => {
            // Shape-locked Phase 3+ stub; the Graph constructor rejects Spill at
            // add_spill_node, so encountering one here would be a corruption.
            panic!(
                "propagate_from_cells: encountered Node::Spill — Phase 3+ feature, must \
                 never appear in Phase 0"
            );
        }
    }
}

   852	    /// volatile formula (NOW / RAND / TODAY / RANDBETWEEN / RANDARRAY
   853	    /// / INDIRECT / OFFSET / INFO / CELL, per `is_volatile_function`)

   862	        let volatile: Vec<NodeId> = self.volatile_formulas.iter().copied().collect();

   922	    /// **Phase 3.4 acceptance (SCH-3-01..04 entry point).** Atomically
   923	    /// claim the dirty set and run the Phase 0 W3-3 iterative Tarjan
   924	    /// scheduler over it. The returned [`Schedule`] partitions nodes:
   925	    ///
   926	    /// - `sorted`: dependency-first topological order. Evaluate
   927	    ///   `sorted[0]` before `sorted[1]`, etc.
   928	    /// - `cycled`: nodes in a non-trivial strongly connected component
   929	    ///   (size > 1) or with a self-loop. Per Phase 0 spec these get
   930	    ///   `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ)`.
   931	    ///
   932	    /// The dirty set is cleared by this call. Edges to non-dirty
   933	    /// nodes are not traversed (those nodes aren't being recomputed;
   934	    /// their current values are read as-is). Input ordering is
   935	    /// sorted-by-NodeId before the scheduler runs so SCH-3-03
   936	    /// determinism holds: same workbook + same edits → same schedule
   937	    /// across runs (the underlying scheduler is already deterministic
   938	    /// in its input slice order; we just normalize HashSet iteration).
   939	    pub fn schedule_dirty(&mut self) -> Schedule {
   940	        let mut dirty_vec: Vec<NodeId> = self.dirty.drain().collect();
   941	        dirty_vec.sort();
   942	        schedule(&self.graph, &dirty_vec)
   943	    }
   944	}

   171	    /// Register that `formula_node` depends on cells in `range`. Atomically updates BOTH
   172	    /// the stripe index AND `formula_to_range_deps`. Callers should invoke this once per
   173	    /// range reference in a formula's AST (a formula like `=SUM(A:A) + COUNT(B:B)` calls
   174	    /// register_range_dependency twice).
   175	    ///
   176	    /// The Phase 0 W3-5 callers (Week 4 `ql-exec` binder) own:
   177	    /// 1. Calling `add_cell_node` / `add_formula_region_node` for the formula's owning vertex.
   178	    /// 2. Calling `register_range_dependency` for each range the formula references.
   179	    /// 3. Optionally adding explicit edges via `add_edge` for direct cell-to-cell deps.
   180	    pub fn register_range_dependency(
   181	        &mut self,
   182	        formula_node: NodeId,
   183	        range: RangeRef,
   184	        sheet_id: SheetId,
   185	    ) {
   186	        assert!(
   187	            formula_node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
   188	            "Graph::register_range_dependency: formula_node {} out of bounds",
   189	            formula_node.index()
   190	        );
   191	        let inserts_before = self.stripes.total_insertions();
   192	        self.stripes.register(formula_node, &range, sheet_id);
   193	        let inserts_after = self.stripes.total_insertions();
   194	        // `stripe_inserts` counts the NEW insertions this call produced. HashSet dedup
   195	        // means a re-registration of the same (formula_node, range) might be a no-op,
   196	        // in which case the delta is zero — matches Formualizer's instrumentation
   197	        // semantics (`engine/graph/range_deps.rs:78-86`).
   198	        self.stats.stripe_inserts = self
   199	            .stats
   200	            .stripe_inserts
   201	            .checked_add((inserts_after - inserts_before) as u64)
   202	            .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: stripe_inserts overflowed u64");
   203	        self.formula_to_range_deps
   204	            .entry(formula_node)
   205	            .or_default()
   206	            .push(range);
   207	        self.revision = self
   208	            .revision
   209	            .checked_add(1)
   210	            .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: revision counter overflowed u64");
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    /// Borrow the instrumentation counters. Used by tests + the W3-7 graph-profile.json
   214	    /// export (OG-06 acceptance).
   215	    pub fn stats(&self) -> GraphStats {
   216	        self.stats
   217	    }
   218	
   219	    /// Bump `chunked_reduce_chunks_processed` by `n`. Phase 0 doesn't increment this
   220	    /// from inside ql-calcgraph; Week 4 ql-exec calls this on each chunk it processes
   221	    /// during a `SUM`/`COUNT`/etc. reduction. Locking the API in W3-6 means Week 4 just
   222	    /// wires the increments without a graph-shape change.
   223	    pub fn record_chunked_reduce(&mut self, chunks_processed: u64) {
   224	        self.stats.chunked_reduce_chunks_processed = self
   225	            .stats
   226	            .chunked_reduce_chunks_processed
   227	            .checked_add(chunks_processed)
   228	            .expect("Graph::record_chunked_reduce: counter overflowed u64");
   229	    }
   230	
   231	    /// True dependents of a cell write at `(sheet, row, col)`. Two-pass:
   232	    /// 1. Stripe lookup → candidate formula nodes (CORR-21 coarse-grained).
   233	    /// 2. Precision filter against each candidate's `formula_to_range_deps` — drops
   234	    ///    candidates whose actual range doesn't contain the cell.
   235	    ///
   236	    /// Returns the precise dependent set, **sorted ascending by `NodeId`** so callers
   237	    /// receive deterministic ordering across runs. Audit H2 (2026-05-12): the previous
   238	    /// implementation collected from a `HashSet` which leaked Rust's randomized hash
   239	    /// state — two identical edits on the same workbook would produce different
   240	    /// downstream scheduling order, silently breaking replay determinism.
   241	    pub fn dependents_for_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Vec<NodeId> {
   242	        let candidates = self.stripes.candidates_for_cell(sheet, row, col);
   243	        let mut out: Vec<NodeId> = candidates
   244	            .into_iter()
   245	            .filter(|formula_id| {
   246	                self.formula_to_range_deps
   247	                    .get(formula_id)
   248	                    .is_some_and(|ranges| ranges.iter().any(|r| range_contains_rowcol(r, row, col)))
   249	            })
   250	            .collect();
   251	        out.sort();
   252	        out
   253	    }
   254	
   255	    /// Total registered range-dependencies across all formula nodes. Counts each
   256	    /// `register_range_dependency` call. Used by tests + W3-7 graph-profile export.
   257	    pub fn range_dependency_count(&self) -> usize {
   258	        self.formula_to_range_deps.values().map(Vec::len).sum()
   259	    }

 succeeded in 0ms:
    80	/// non-trivial strongly connected components (size > 1) plus all self-loops (size 1 + an
    81	/// edge to self).
    82	///
    83	/// Edges to nodes outside `dirty_subset` are not traversed — those nodes aren't being
    84	/// re-evaluated, just read.
    85	///
    86	/// Duplicate entries in `dirty_subset` are tolerated (deduped internally via a HashSet).
    87	pub fn schedule(graph: &Graph, dirty_subset: &[NodeId]) -> Schedule {
    88	    let dirty: HashSet<NodeId> = dirty_subset.iter().copied().collect();
    89	    if dirty.is_empty() {
    90	        return Schedule::default();
    91	    }
    92	
    93	    let mut indices: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    94	    let mut lowlinks: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    95	    let mut on_scc_stack: HashMap<NodeId, bool> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    96	    let mut scc_stack: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
    97	    let mut next_index: u32 = 0;
    98	
    99	    let mut sorted: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   100	    let mut cycled: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   101	
   102	    // Visit every node in the dirty subset. Iteration order is from the input slice (not
   103	    // the HashSet's randomized order) so callers get deterministic schedules from
   104	    // deterministic inputs. Deduplicated via `indices.contains_key`.
   105	    for &seed in dirty_subset {
   106	        if indices.contains_key(&seed) {
   107	            continue;
   108	        }
   109	        tarjan_dfs(
   110	            seed,
   111	            graph,
   112	            &dirty,
   113	            &mut indices,
   114	            &mut lowlinks,
   115	            &mut on_scc_stack,
   116	            &mut scc_stack,
   117	            &mut next_index,
   118	            &mut sorted,
   119	            &mut cycled,
   120	        );
   121	    }
   122	
   123	    Schedule { sorted, cycled }
   124	}
   125	
   126	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   127	fn tarjan_dfs(
   128	    seed: NodeId,
   129	    graph: &Graph,
   130	    dirty: &HashSet<NodeId>,
   131	    indices: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   132	    lowlinks: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   133	    on_scc_stack: &mut HashMap<NodeId, bool>,
   134	    scc_stack: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   135	    next_index: &mut u32,
   136	    sorted: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   137	    cycled: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   138	) {
   139	    // Explicit DFS frame: (node, child index in outgoing(node) — within-dirty filter).
   140	    // We compute the child slice once at first visit + iterate it index-by-index across
   141	    // frames. Filtering "is this child in dirty?" happens at child-visit time so we don't
   142	    // pre-allocate filtered child vectors per node (the hot path under no-cycles is one
   143	    // outgoing-slice scan per node, period).
   144	    let mut dfs_stack: Vec<(NodeId, usize)> = Vec::new();
   145	
   146	    // First visit of seed.
   147	    visit_first(seed, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   148	    dfs_stack.push((seed, 0));
   149	
   150	    while let Some(&(v, child_idx)) = dfs_stack.last() {
   151	        let children = graph.outgoing(v);
   152	
   153	        // Find the next not-yet-considered child that's in the dirty subset.
   154	        let mut next_child: Option<NodeId> = None;
   155	        let mut new_child_idx = child_idx;
   156	        while new_child_idx < children.len() {
   157	            let candidate = children[new_child_idx];
   158	            new_child_idx += 1;
   159	            if dirty.contains(&candidate) {
   160	                next_child = Some(candidate);
   161	                break;
   162	            }
   163	        }
   164	
   165	        // Update this frame's child cursor regardless of whether we recurse or finish.
   166	        let frame_idx = dfs_stack.len() - 1;
   167	        dfs_stack[frame_idx].1 = new_child_idx;
   168	
   169	        if let Some(w) = next_child {
   170	            if !indices.contains_key(&w) {
   171	                // Unvisited dirty child — recurse.
   172	                visit_first(w, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   173	                dfs_stack.push((w, 0));
   174	            } else if *on_scc_stack
   175	                .get(&w)
   176	                .expect("topo invariant: every key in `indices` must also be in `on_scc_stack` — visit_first populates both atomically")
   177	            {
   178	                // Phase 2A.7 audit M13: switched from `unwrap_or(&false)` to `.expect(...)`.
   179	                // The prior fallback silently treated "missing key" as "not on stack",
   180	                // conflating "not yet visited" with "explicitly off-stack" — masking a
   181	                // Tarjan invariant violation. Per no-fallbacks rule, an invariant
   182	                // violation must panic loudly, not produce subtly-wrong SCC results.
   183	                //
   184	                // Back-edge or cross-edge to a node currently on the SCC stack — update
   185	                // v's lowlink with w's index.
   186	                let w_idx = indices[&w];
   187	                let v_low = lowlinks[&v];
   188	                if w_idx < v_low {
   189	                    lowlinks.insert(v, w_idx);
   190	                }
   191	            }
   192	            // Else: w is in a completed SCC; ignore the cross-edge.
   193	        } else {
   194	            // All children processed — post-order completion.
   195	            dfs_stack.pop();
   196	
   197	            // If v is the root of an SCC, pop it off the SCC stack.
   198	            if lowlinks[&v] == indices[&v] {
   199	                let mut scc: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   200	                loop {
   201	                    let w = scc_stack
   202	                        .pop()
   203	                        .expect("scc_stack drained mid-pop — algorithm invariant violated");
   204	                    on_scc_stack.insert(w, false);
   205	                    scc.push(w);
   206	                    if w == v {
   207	                        break;
   208	                    }
   209	                }
   210	
   211	                // Classify the SCC.
   212	                let is_cycle = scc.len() > 1
   213	                    || (scc.len() == 1
   214	                        && graph
   215	                            .outgoing(scc[0])
   216	                            .iter()
   217	                            .any(|&t| t == scc[0] && dirty.contains(&t)));
   218	                if is_cycle {
   219	                    cycled.extend(scc);
   220	                } else {
   221	                    // Single acyclic node — append to sorted.
   222	                    sorted.extend(scc);
   223	                }
   224	            }
   225	

codex
**Verdict**

Your preliminary view is mostly right: **GAP-G-01 and GAP-G-03 are independent correctness failures, and the smallest credible ship-now decision is A + a narrow C-like scheduler augmentation.** I would not route either through FormulaRegion binder now.

**1. Independence**

They are independent:

- **G-01** is stale stored state: direct `graph.add_edge()` calls and `register_range_dependency()` survive formula rebind because `remove_formula_deps()` only clears session-side maps, explicitly not graph edges/stripes. See [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:440) and [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:519).
- **G-03** is missing scheduler visibility: `register_range_dependency()` updates stripes and `formula_to_range_deps`, but never scheduler adjacency. Tarjan only walks `graph.outgoing(v)`. See [graph.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/graph.rs:180) and [topo.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/topo.rs:151).

Option A does not solve G-03. Option C does not solve G-01. They do not meaningfully regress each other if implemented cleanly. In fact, A makes C safer because C must consume only live range deps; stale `formula_to_range_deps` would otherwise create temporary stale scheduler edges.

**2. Option C Correctness**

Option C is correct in principle, with one required precision detail: **the temp-edge range containment check must include sheet resolution**, not just `range_contains_rowcol(range, row, col)`, because that helper intentionally ignores sheet and relies on stripe prefiltering today.

The correct shape is:

- Take sorted dirty nodes before draining.
- For each dirty formula `F`, inspect live `formula_to_range_deps[F]`.
- For each dirty node `D` that is a formula cell, get `(sheet,row,col)`.
- If the range contains `(sheet,row,col)`, expose a temporary edge `F -> D` to Tarjan.
- Do not create edges to non-formula literal cells; they are already current values and do not participate in scheduling.

Partial dirty sets are acceptable only under the existing invariant: **dirty propagation must mark every formula whose recomputed value can affect another dirty formula**. For name changes, your reasoning is right after H2: `on_set_name` now seeds the directly affected formula and fans out via `mark_dirty_from_cell_write()`, which uses both range and direct reverse indices at [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:781). If that invariant is violated elsewhere, C cannot fix it.

I would implement C as `schedule_dirty_with_range_deps()` in `CalcgraphSession` or `topo::schedule_with_extra_edges`, not by mutating `Graph`.

**3. Option A API**

Yes, `clear_outgoing(node)` must also remove reverse pointers from each former target’s `incoming[target]`. Otherwise dirty propagation and any code using reverse adjacency remains stale. With current `Vec<Vec<NodeId>>`, linear removal from target incoming lists is acceptable for the rebind path.

I would add:

- `AdjacencyVectors::clear_outgoing(from) -> Vec<NodeId>`
- `Graph::clear_outgoing(from)`
- `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula)`
- `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula(formula)` backed by `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>`

Also consider dedup while clearing, because the current graph permits duplicate edges. If `outgoing[from]` has duplicate `to`, remove the same count from `incoming[to]` or remove all matching entries consistently.

Determinism risk is manageable. Re-extraction order is deterministic if `ExprPlan` traversal is deterministic. The new ordering after clear + re-add should match the same formula plan’s old order.

**4. Option D**

I would not choose epoch-marked edges. It keeps dead state on the hot scheduler path and complicates every traversal.

`IndexSet<NodeId>` is plausible but probably not worth adding now. The current design intentionally avoids new deps and preserves insertion order with `Vec`. Rebind removal is not hot enough to justify changing the adjacency representation.

Rebuilding scheduler edges from bound plans every `recompute_dirty` is also the wrong trade: it avoids stale graph state, but pays repeated bind/dependency work and duplicates logic already captured in session/graph state.

Best Option D variant: **add a generic scheduler edge-provider abstraction**:

```rust
base graph outgoing + session-provided temporary range outgoing
```

That lets C avoid mutating graph storage and keeps Tarjan reusable. But it is still basically Option C, just cleaner.

**5. Effort Estimate**

I think W5-47’s “3-5 days” is conservative. For direct G-01 revocation only, this is closer to **one focused session**:

- adjacency clear API
- stripe reverse index
- graph range clear API
- call from `extract_and_register_deps()` before re-register
- tests for stale direct edge false cycle and stale range dirtying

The reason it might grow is test fallout: some Phase 0 tests currently assert append-only/no-dedup behavior. But I did not see a deep invariant that needs append-only for correctness. The append-only contract is documented, not structurally essential.

C is another **half to one session**, mostly because the scheduler needs a clean way to see temporary edges and tests need to cover cycles, ordering, sheet isolation, and partial dirty sets.

**6. Stop Conditions**

Defer A+C only if one of these turns out true during implementation:

- Phase 4.3 V2 range functions require FormulaRegion for semantics, not just performance. I see no evidence they do; SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup need correct range dependencies, not region grouping.
- Temp-edge C cannot be made sheet-precise without exposing too much graph internals. That would mean add the minimal accessor first, not jump to B.
- `formula_to_stripe_keys` memory is material at scale. Watch whole-column formulas: it is one key per touched stripe, not per cell, so it should stay linear in registered ranges.
- `clear_outgoing` shows a real high-in-degree perf cliff in benchmarks. This is rebind/edit-time, not OG-02 recompute hot path, so I would require measured evidence before blocking.
- A test reveals stale retroactive edges from “dep becomes formula” cannot be revoked with only `clear_outgoing(formula)`. I think it can, but this is the main area to test carefully.

**Implementation Watch List**

Ship **A first**, because C must rely on live range deps. Then ship C.

Regression tests I would require:

- `B1=A1`, rebind `B1=1`, then `A1=B1` does not produce `#CIRC!`.
- `SUM(A:A)` rebind to `SUM(B:B)`; edit `A1` no longer dirties the formula.
- `A1=SUM(BRange)`, `B1=A1+1` produces `#CIRC!`.
- Dirty ordering: range formula waits for dirty formula inside its range.
- Sheet isolation for temp range edges.
- Same formula rebind twice gives stable schedule order.
